<p><b>Brunei</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;b&#39; in &#39;buy&#39;">b</span><span title="&#39;r&#39; in &#39;rye&#39;">r</span><span title="/uː/: &#39;oo&#39; in &#39;goose&#39;">uː</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/aɪ/: &#39;i&#39; in &#39;tide&#39;">aɪ</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span class="nowrap" style="font-size:85%">(<span class="unicode haudio"><span class="fn"><span style="white-space:nowrap;margin-right:.25em;"><a href="/wiki/File:En-us-Brunei.ogg" title="About this sound"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/11px-Loudspeaker.svg.png" decoding="async" width="11" height="11" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/17px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/22px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="20" data-file-height="20" /></a></span><a href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/1/19/En-us-Brunei.ogg" class="internal" title="En-us-Brunei.ogg">listen</a></span></span>)</span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling">broo-<span style="font-size:90%">NY</span></i></a>, <small>Malay:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="ms-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Malay" title="Help:IPA/Malay">[brunaɪ]</a></span>&#32;<span class="nowrap" style="font-size:85%">(<span class="unicode haudio"><span class="fn"><span style="white-space:nowrap;margin-right:.25em;"><a href="/wiki/File:Brunei-pronunciation-ms.ogg" title="About this sound"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/11px-Loudspeaker.svg.png" decoding="async" width="11" height="11" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/17px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/22px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="20" data-file-height="20" /></a></span><a href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/05/Brunei-pronunciation-ms.ogg" class="internal" title="Brunei-pronunciation-ms.ogg">listen</a></span></span>)</span>), formally <b>Brunei Darussalam</b><sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup> (<a href="/wiki/Malay_language" title="Malay language">Malay</a>: <i lang="ms">Negara Brunei Darussalam</i>, <a href="/wiki/Jawi_alphabet" class="mw-redirect" title="Jawi alphabet">Jawi</a>: <span title="Malay (macrolanguage)-language text"><span lang="ms-Arab" dir="rtl"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1116685952"/><span class="script-arabic script-Arab" dir="rtl" style="font-size: 125%;">نݢارا بروني دارالسلام</span>&#8206;</span></span>, <abbr style="font-size:85%" title="literal translation">lit.</abbr><span style="white-space: nowrap;">&#8201;</span>'Nation&#160;of Brunei, the&#160;Abode of Peace&#39;<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>), is a country located on the north coast of the island of <a href="/wiki/Borneo" title="Borneo">Borneo</a> in <a href="/wiki/Southeast_Asia" title="Southeast Asia">Southeast Asia</a>. Apart from its <a href="/wiki/South_China_Sea" title="South China Sea">South China Sea</a> coast, it is completely surrounded by the <a href="/wiki/Malaysian_state" class="mw-redirect" title="Malaysian state">Malaysian state</a> of <a href="/wiki/Sarawak" title="Sarawak">Sarawak</a>. It is separated into two parts by the Sarawak district of <a href="/wiki/Limbang_District" title="Limbang District">Limbang</a>. Brunei is the only sovereign state entirely on Borneo; the remainder of the island is divided between <a href="/wiki/Malaysia" title="Malaysia">Malaysia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Indonesia" title="Indonesia">Indonesia</a>. As of 2020<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Brunei&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, its population was 460,345,<sup id="cite_ref-depd.gov.bn_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-depd.gov.bn-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> of whom about 100,000 live in the <a href="/wiki/Capital_city" title="Capital city">capital</a> and largest city, <a href="/wiki/Bandar_Seri_Begawan" title="Bandar Seri Begawan">Bandar Seri Begawan</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Brunei" title="Government of Brunei">government</a> is an absolute monarchy ruled by its <a href="/wiki/Sultan_of_Brunei" class="mw-redirect" title="Sultan of Brunei">Sultan</a>, entitled the <a href="/wiki/Yang_di-Pertuan_Negara" title="Yang di-Pertuan Negara">Yang di-Pertuan</a>, and implements a combination of <a href="/wiki/English_common_law" class="mw-redirect" title="English common law">English common law</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sharia_law" class="mw-redirect" title="Sharia law">sharia law</a>, as well as general <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islamic</a> practices.
</p><p>At the peak of the <a href="/wiki/Bruneian_Empire" class="mw-redirect" title="Bruneian Empire">Bruneian Empire</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bolkiah" title="Bolkiah">Sultan Bolkiah</a> (reigned 1485–1528) is claimed to have had control over most regions of <a href="/wiki/Borneo" title="Borneo">Borneo</a>, including modern-day Sarawak and <a href="/wiki/Sabah" title="Sabah">Sabah</a>, as well as the <a href="/wiki/Sulu_Archipelago" title="Sulu Archipelago">Sulu Archipelago</a> off the northeast tip of Borneo, and the islands off the northwest tip of Borneo. Claims also state that they had control over <a href="/wiki/Seludong" class="mw-redirect" title="Seludong">Seludong</a> (or the Kingdom of Maynila, where the modern-day Philippine capital <a href="/wiki/Manila" title="Manila">Manila</a> now stands) but Southeast Asian scholars believe this refers to a settlement Mount Selurong in Indonesia.<sup id="cite_ref-Abinales2005_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Abinales2005-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> The maritime state of Brunei was visited by Spain's <a href="/wiki/Timeline_of_Magellan%27s_circumnavigation" class="mw-redirect" title="Timeline of Magellan&#39;s circumnavigation">Magellan Expedition</a> in 1521 and fought against Spain in the 1578 <a href="/wiki/Castilian_War" title="Castilian War">Castilian War</a>.
</p><p>During the 19th century, the Bruneian Empire began to decline. The Sultanate ceded Sarawak (<a href="/wiki/Kuching" title="Kuching">Kuching</a>) to <a href="/wiki/James_Brooke" title="James Brooke">James Brooke</a> and installed him as the <a href="/wiki/White_Rajahs" title="White Rajahs">White Rajah</a>, and it ceded Sabah to the British <a href="/wiki/North_Borneo_Chartered_Company" title="North Borneo Chartered Company">North Borneo Chartered Company</a>. In 1888, Brunei became a <a href="/wiki/British_protectorate" title="British protectorate">British protectorate</a> and was assigned a <a href="/wiki/Resident_(title)" class="mw-redirect" title="Resident (title)">British resident</a> as colonial manager in 1906. After the Japanese occupation during <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, in 1959 a new constitution was written. In 1962, a small <a href="/wiki/Brunei_revolt" title="Brunei revolt">armed rebellion</a> against the monarchy was ended with the help of the British.
</p><p>Brunei has been led by the <a href="/wiki/List_of_sultans_of_Brunei" title="List of sultans of Brunei">Sultan of Brunei</a> <a href="/wiki/Hassanal_Bolkiah" title="Hassanal Bolkiah">Hassanal Bolkiah</a> since 1967, and had gained its independence as a British <a href="/wiki/Protectorate" title="Protectorate">protectorate</a> on 1 January 1984. The country is an <a href="/wiki/Autocratic" class="mw-redirect" title="Autocratic">autocratic</a> <a href="/wiki/Absolute_monarchy" title="Absolute monarchy">absolute monarchy</a>. Economic growth during the 1990s and 2000s, with the GDP increasing 56% from 1999 to 2008, transformed Brunei into an industrialised country. It has developed wealth from extensive petroleum and natural gas fields. Brunei has the second-highest <a href="/wiki/Human_Development_Index" title="Human Development Index">Human Development Index</a> among the <a href="/wiki/Southeast_Asian" class="mw-redirect" title="Southeast Asian">Southeast Asian</a> nations, after <a href="/wiki/Singapore" title="Singapore">Singapore</a>. According to the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a> (IMF), Brunei is <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(PPP)_per_capita" title="List of countries by GDP (PPP) per capita">ranked fifth in the world by gross domestic product per capita at purchasing power parity</a>. The IMF estimated in 2011 that Brunei was one of two countries (the other being <a href="/wiki/History_of_Libya_under_Muammar_Gaddafi#Libyan_Arab_Jamahiriya_(1977–2011)" title="History of Libya under Muammar Gaddafi">Libya</a>) with a public debt at 0% of the national GDP.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span></h2>
<p>According to local historiography, Brunei was founded by Awang Alak Betatar, later to be <a href="/wiki/Muhammad_Shah_of_Brunei" title="Muhammad Shah of Brunei">Sultan Muhammad Shah</a>, reigning around AD 1400. He moved from Garang in the <a href="/wiki/Temburong" class="mw-redirect" title="Temburong">Temburong</a> District<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200926_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200926-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> to the <a href="/wiki/Brunei_River" title="Brunei River">Brunei River</a> estuary, discovering Brunei. According to legend, upon landing he exclaimed, <i>Baru nah</i> (loosely translated as "that's it!" or "there"), from which the name "Brunei" was derived.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEde_Vienne201627_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEde_Vienne201627-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> He was the first Muslim ruler of Brunei.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Before the rise of the <a href="/wiki/Bruneian_Empire" class="mw-redirect" title="Bruneian Empire">Bruneian Empire</a> under the Muslim Bolkiah Dynasty, Brunei is believed to have been under Buddhist rulers.<sup id="cite_ref-Nicholl1980_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nicholl1980-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It was renamed "Barunai" in the 14th century, possibly influenced by the <a href="/wiki/Sanskrit" title="Sanskrit">Sanskrit</a> word "<i><span title="Sanskrit-language romanization"><i lang="sa-Latn">varuṇ</i></span></i>" (<span title="Sanskrit-language text"><span lang="sa">वरुण</span></span>), meaning "seafarers".<sup id="cite_ref-Minahan2012_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Minahan2012-24">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> The word "<a href="/wiki/Borneo" title="Borneo">Borneo</a>" is of the same origin. In the country's full name, <i><span title="Malay (macrolanguage)-language text"><i lang="ms">Negara Brunei Darussalam</i></span></i>, <i><span title="Malay (macrolanguage)-language text"><i lang="ms">darussalam</i></span></i> (<a href="/wiki/Arabic_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Arabic language">Arabic</a>: <span lang="ar" dir="rtl">دار السلام</span>) means "abode of peace", while <i><span title="Malay (macrolanguage)-language text"><i lang="ms">negara</i></span></i> means "country" in <a href="/wiki/Malay_language" title="Malay language">Malay</a>. A shortened version of the Malay official name, "Brunei Darussalam", has also entered common usage, particularly in official contexts, and is present in the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Group_of_Experts_on_Geographical_Names" title="United Nations Group of Experts on Geographical Names">United Nations Group of Experts on Geographical Names</a> geographical database,<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> as well as the official ASEAN<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> and Commonwealth<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> listings.
</p><p>The earliest recorded documentation by the West about Brunei is by an Italian known as <a href="/wiki/Ludovico_di_Varthema" title="Ludovico di Varthema">Ludovico di Varthema</a>. On his documentation back to 1550;
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>We arrived at the island of Bornei<i> (Brunei or Borneo), </i>which is distant from the <i>Maluch</i> about two hundred miles [three hundred kilometres], and we found that it was somewhat larger than the aforesaid and much lower. The people are pagans and are men of goodwill. Their colour is whiter than that of the other sort&#160;... in this island justice is well administered&#160;...<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Brunei" title="History of Brunei">History of Brunei</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_history">Early history</span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1034237262">.mw-parser-output .stack{box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .stack>div{margin:1px;overflow:hidden}@media all and (min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .stack-clear-left{float:left;clear:left}.mw-parser-output .stack-clear-right{float:right;clear:right}.mw-parser-output .stack-left{float:left}.mw-parser-output .stack-right{float:right}.mw-parser-output .stack-margin-clear-left{float:left;clear:left;margin-right:1em}.mw-parser-output .stack-margin-clear-right{float:right;clear:right;margin-left:1em}.mw-parser-output .stack-margin-left{float:left;margin-right:1em}.mw-parser-output .stack-margin-right{float:right;margin-left:1em}}</style><div class="stack stack-left"><div><div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Tomb_of_the_King_of_Boni_(Brunei)_Nanjing.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/The_Tomb_of_the_King_of_Boni_%28Brunei%29_Nanjing.JPG/220px-The_Tomb_of_the_King_of_Boni_%28Brunei%29_Nanjing.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/The_Tomb_of_the_King_of_Boni_%28Brunei%29_Nanjing.JPG/330px-The_Tomb_of_the_King_of_Boni_%28Brunei%29_Nanjing.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/The_Tomb_of_the_King_of_Boni_%28Brunei%29_Nanjing.JPG/440px-The_Tomb_of_the_King_of_Boni_%28Brunei%29_Nanjing.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3072" data-file-height="2304" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Tomb_of_the_King_of_Boni_(Brunei)_Nanjing.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Tomb_of_the_King_of_Boni" title="Tomb of the King of Boni">tomb of a ruler of Boni</a> in <a href="/wiki/Nanjing" title="Nanjing">Nanjing</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jiangsu" title="Jiangsu">Jiangsu</a>, China</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>The settlement known as Vijayapura was a vassal-state to the Buddhist <a href="/wiki/Srivijaya" title="Srivijaya">Srivijaya</a> empire and was thought to be located in Borneo's Northwest which flourished in the 7th Century.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> This alternate Srivijaya referring to Brunei, was known to Arabic sources as "Sribuza".<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> The Arabic author Al Ya'akubi writing in 800 recorded that the kingdom of Musa (Muja, which is old Brunei) was in alliance with the kingdom of Mayd (Either <a href="/wiki/Ma-i" title="Ma-i">Ma-i</a> or <a href="/wiki/Madja-as" title="Madja-as">Madja-as</a> in the Philippines), against the Chinese Empire which they waged war against.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> In the aftermath of the Indian <a href="/wiki/Chola_invasion_of_Srivijaya" title="Chola invasion of Srivijaya">Chola invasion of Srivijaya</a>, Datu Puti lead some dissident datus from Sumatra and Borneo in a rebellion against Rajah Makatunao who was a Chola appointed local Rajah or the descendant of Seri Maharajah (In Chinese records). The dissidents and their retinue tried to revive Srivijaya in a new country called <a href="/wiki/Kedatuan_of_Madja-as" class="mw-redirect" title="Kedatuan of Madja-as">Madja-as</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Visayas" title="Visayas">Visayas</a> (an island archipelago named after Srivijaya) in the Philippines. After the 10 Datus established many towns in <a href="/wiki/Panay" title="Panay">Panay</a> and Southern Luzon, according to Augustinian Friar Rev. Fr. Santaren recording in the Spanish era of this Pre-Spanish legendary history, that Datu Macatunao or Rajah Makatunao who was the "sultan of the Moros," and a relative of Datu Puti who seized the properties and riches of the ten datus was eventually killed by the warriors named Labaodungon and Paybare, after learning of this injustice from their father-in-law Paiburong, sailed to Odtojan in Borneo where Makatunaw ruled. The warriors sacked the city, killed Makatunaw and his family, retrieved the stolen properties of the 10 datus, enslaved the remaining population of Odtojan, and sailed back to Panay. Labaw Donggon and his wife, Ojaytanayon, later settled in a place called Moroboro.<sup id="cite_ref-Talaguit_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Talaguit-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> One of the earliest Chinese records of an independent kingdom in Borneo is the 977 AD letter to the Chinese emperor from the ruler of Boni, which some scholars believe to refer to Borneo.<sup id="cite_ref-Hutton2000_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hutton2000-33">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> The Bruneians regained their independence from Srivijaya due to the onset of a Javanese-Sumatran war.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> In 1225, the Chinese official <a href="/wiki/Zhao_Rukuo" title="Zhao Rukuo">Zhao Rukuo</a> reported that Boni had 100 warships to protect its trade, and that there was great wealth in the kingdom.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200943_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200943-35">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> Marco Polo suggested in his memoirs that the Great Khan or the ruler of the Mongol Empire, attempted and failed many times in invading "Great Java" which was the European name for Bruneian controlled Borneo.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs additional references to reliable sources. (December 2021)">additional citation(s) needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> In the 1300s the Chinese annals, <i>Nanhai zhi</i>, reported that Brunei invaded or administered <a href="/wiki/Sarawak" title="Sarawak">Sarawak</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sabah" title="Sabah">Sabah</a> as well as the Philippine kingdoms of <a href="/wiki/Rajahnate_of_Butuan" class="mw-redirect" title="Rajahnate of Butuan">Butuan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sultanate_of_Sulu" title="Sultanate of Sulu">Sulu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ma-i" title="Ma-i">Ma-i</a> (Mindoro), Malilu 麻裏蘆 (present-day <a href="/wiki/Manila" title="Manila">Manila</a>), <a href="/wiki/Sanmalan" title="Sanmalan">Shahuchong</a> 沙胡重 (present-day Siocon or <a href="/wiki/Zamboanga_City" title="Zamboanga City">Zamboanga</a>), Yachen 啞陳 <a href="/wiki/Oton" title="Oton">Oton</a> (Part of the <a href="/wiki/Madja-as" title="Madja-as">Madja-as</a> Kedatuan), and 文杜陵 Wenduling (present-day <a href="/wiki/Sultanate_of_Maguindanao" title="Sultanate of Maguindanao">Mindanao</a>),<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> which would regain their independence at a later date.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the 14th century, the <a href="/wiki/Java" title="Java">Javanese</a> manuscript <i><a href="/wiki/Nagarakretagama" title="Nagarakretagama">Nagarakretagama</a></i>, written by <a href="/wiki/Mpu_Prapanca" title="Mpu Prapanca">Prapanca</a> in 1365, mentioned <i>Barune</i> as the constituent state of Hindu <a href="/wiki/Majapahit" title="Majapahit">Majapahit</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> which had to make an annual <a href="/wiki/Tribute" title="Tribute">tribute</a> of 40 <a href="/wiki/Catty" title="Catty">katis</a> of <a href="/wiki/Camphor" title="Camphor">camphor</a>. In 1369, Sulu which was also formerly part of Majapahit, had successfully rebelled and then attacked Boni, and had invaded the Northeast Coast of Borneo<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> and afterwards had looted the capital of its treasure and gold including sacking two sacred pearls. A fleet from Majapahit succeeded in driving away the Sulus, but Boni was left weaker after the attack.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200944_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200944-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> A Chinese report from 1371 described Boni as poor and totally controlled by Majapahit.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200945_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200945-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the 15th century, Boni had seceded from Majapahit and then converted to Islam. Thus transforming into the independent Sultanate of Brunei. Brunei became a <a href="/wiki/Hashemite" class="mw-redirect" title="Hashemite">Hashemite</a> state when she allowed the Arab Emir of Mecca, <a href="/wiki/Sharif_Ali" title="Sharif Ali">Sharif Ali</a>, to become her third sultan. Scholars claim that the power of the <a href="/wiki/Sultanate" class="mw-redirect" title="Sultanate">Sultanate</a> of Brunei was at <a href="/wiki/Bruneian_Empire" class="mw-redirect" title="Bruneian Empire">its peak</a> between the 15th and 17th centuries, with its power extending from northern Borneo to the southern Philippines (Sulu) and even in the northern Philippines (Manila) which Brunei incorporated via territorial acquisition accomplished through royal marriages.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> For political reasons, the historical rulers of Maynila maintained close cognatic ties through intermarriage with the ruling houses of the <a href="/wiki/Bruneian_Empire" class="mw-redirect" title="Bruneian Empire">Sultanate of Brunei</a>, but Brunei's political influence over Maynila is not considered to have extended to military or political rule.<sup id="cite_ref-Junker1998_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Junker1998-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Intermarriage was a common strategy for large <a href="/wiki/Thalassocracy" title="Thalassocracy">thalassocratic states</a> (maritime states) such as Brunei to extend their influence, and for local rulers such as those of Maynila to help strengthen their family claims to nobility.<sup id="cite_ref-Scott1994_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Scott1994-45">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> Sultan Bolkiah had extended Brunei's power to its greatest extent when it conquered Manila and Sulu as he even attempted but failed to conquer the Visayas islands even though Sultan Bolkiah was half-Visayan himself being descended from a Visayan mother and he was famously known as Sultan Ragam "The Singing Captain", his powerful musical voice was a trait he inherited from his Visayan lineage since Visayans were culturally obsessed with singing, with the best <a href="/wiki/Madja-as" title="Madja-as">Visayan singers often also being members of their warrior castes too</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> However, Islamic Brunei's power was not uncontested in Borneo since it had a Hindu rival in a state founded by Indians called <a href="/wiki/Kutai" title="Kutai">Kutai</a> in the south which they overpowered but didn't destroy. Brunei's dominance in the Philippines was also challenged by two Indianized kingdoms, the Rajahanates of <a href="/wiki/Rajahnate_of_Cebu" class="mw-redirect" title="Rajahnate of Cebu">Cebu</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rajahnate_of_Butuan" class="mw-redirect" title="Rajahnate of Butuan">Butuan</a> which were also coincidentally allied with Kutai and were also at war with Brunei's dependencies; Sulu and Manila as well as their mutual ally, the <a href="/wiki/Sultanate_of_Maguindanao" title="Sultanate of Maguindanao">Sultanate of Maguindanao</a>. The Kedatuans of Madja-as and Dapitan were also belligerent against Brunei due to them being the targets of constant Muslim attacks organized from Maguindanao and <a href="/wiki/Sultanate_of_Ternate" title="Sultanate of Ternate">Ternate</a>, a Papuan speaking state in the vicinity of Oceania that grew wealthy by monopolizing spice production. Nevertheless, by the 16th century, Islam was firmly rooted in Brunei, and the country had built one of its biggest mosques. In 1578, Alonso Beltrán, a Spanish traveller, described it as being five stories tall and built on the water.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="War_with_Spain_and_decline">War with Spain and decline</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Brunei_territorial_lose_(1400%E2%80%931890).gif" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Brunei_territorial_lose_%281400%E2%80%931890%29.gif/220px-Brunei_territorial_lose_%281400%E2%80%931890%29.gif" decoding="async" width="220" height="110" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Brunei_territorial_lose_%281400%E2%80%931890%29.gif/330px-Brunei_territorial_lose_%281400%E2%80%931890%29.gif 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Brunei_territorial_lose_%281400%E2%80%931890%29.gif/440px-Brunei_territorial_lose_%281400%E2%80%931890%29.gif 2x" data-file-width="450" data-file-height="225" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Brunei_territorial_lose_(1400%E2%80%931890).gif" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Brunei territorial losses from 1400 to 1890</div></div></div><p> Brunei briefly rose to prominence in Southeast Asia when the Portuguese occupied <a href="/wiki/Malacca" title="Malacca">Malacca</a> and thereby forced the wealthy and powerful but displaced Muslim refugees there to relocate to nearby Sultanates such as <a href="/wiki/Sultanate_of_Aceh" class="mw-redirect" title="Sultanate of Aceh">Aceh</a> and Brunei. The Bruneian Sultan then intervened in a territorial conflict between <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Tondo" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Tondo">Hindu Tondo</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Maynila" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Maynila">Muslim Manila</a> in the Philippines by appointing the Bruneian descended <a href="/wiki/Rajah_Matanda" title="Rajah Matanda">Rajah Ache</a> of Manila as admiral of the Bruneian navy in a rivalry against Tondo and as the enforcer of Bruneian interests in the Philippines. He subsequently encountered the Magellan expedition<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> wherein <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Pigafetta" title="Antonio Pigafetta">Antonio Pigafetta</a> noted that under orders from his grandfather the Sultan of Brunei, Ache had previously sacked the Buddhist city of Loue in Southwest Borneo for being faithful to the old religion and rebelling against the authority of Sultanate.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> However, <a href="/wiki/Colonialism" title="Colonialism">European influence</a> gradually brought an end to Brunei's <a href="/wiki/Regional_power" title="Regional power">regional power</a>, as Brunei entered a period of decline compounded by internal strife over royal succession. In the face of these invasions by European Christian powers, the <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Empire" title="Ottoman Empire">Ottoman Caliphate</a> aided the beleaguered Southeast Asian Sultanates by making <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_expedition_to_Aceh" class="mw-redirect" title="Ottoman expedition to Aceh">Aceh</a> a <a href="/wiki/Protectorate" title="Protectorate">protectorate</a> and sending expeditions to reinforce, train and equip the local <a href="/wiki/Mujahideen" title="Mujahideen">mujahideen</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Turks were routinely migrating to Brunei as evidenced by the complaints of Manila Oidor Melchor Davalos who in his 1585 report, say that Turks were coming to Sumatra, Borneo and Ternate every year, including defeated veterans from the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Lepanto" title="Battle of Lepanto">Battle of Lepanto</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> The presence of Turks assisting Brunei against Habsburg Spain, makes the subsequent <a href="/wiki/Castille_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Castille War">Castille War</a> a part of the <a href="/wiki/Ottoman%E2%80%93Habsburg_wars" title="Ottoman–Habsburg wars">Ottoman–Habsburg wars</a>.
</p><p>Spain <a href="/wiki/Castille_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Castille War">declared war</a> in 1578, planning to attack and capture <a href="/wiki/Kota_Batu,_Brunei-Muara" class="mw-redirect" title="Kota Batu, Brunei-Muara">Kota Batu</a>, Brunei's capital at the time. This was based in part on the assistance of two Bruneian <a href="/wiki/Pengiran#Brunei" class="mw-redirect" title="Pengiran">noblemen</a>, Pengiran Seri Lela and Pengiran Seri Ratna. The former had travelled to <a href="/wiki/Manila" title="Manila">Manila</a>, then the centre of the Spanish colony. Manila itself was captured from Brunei, Christianised and made a territory of the <a href="/wiki/Viceroyalty_of_New_Spain" class="mw-redirect" title="Viceroyalty of New Spain">Viceroyalty of New Spain</a> which was centered in Mexico City. Pengiran Seri Lela came to offer Brunei as a <a href="/wiki/Tributary_state" title="Tributary state">tributary</a> to Spain for help to recover the throne usurped by his brother, Saiful Rijal.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> The Spanish agreed that if they succeeded in conquering Brunei, Pengiran Seri Lela would be appointed as the sultan, while Pengiran Seri Ratna would be the new <a href="/wiki/Bendahara" title="Bendahara">Bendahara</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Control_of_the_island_of_Borneo.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/Control_of_the_island_of_Borneo.png/220px-Control_of_the_island_of_Borneo.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="245" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/Control_of_the_island_of_Borneo.png/330px-Control_of_the_island_of_Borneo.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/Control_of_the_island_of_Borneo.png/440px-Control_of_the_island_of_Borneo.png 2x" data-file-width="1080" data-file-height="1202" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Control_of_the_island_of_Borneo.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Boundaries of Brunei (green) since 1890</div></div></div>
<p>In March 1578, a fresh Spanish fleet had arrived from Mexico and settled at the Philippines, they were led by <a href="/wiki/Francisco_de_Sande" title="Francisco de Sande">De Sande</a>, acting as <a href="/wiki/Capit%C3%A1n-General" class="mw-redirect" title="Capitán-General">Capitán-General</a>, he organised an expedition from Manila for Brunei. The expedition consisted of 400 Spaniards and Mexicans, 1,500 <a href="/wiki/Filipino_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Filipino people">Filipino</a> natives and 300 Borneans.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> The campaign was one of many, which also included action in <a href="/wiki/Mindanao" title="Mindanao">Mindanao</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sulu_Archipelago" title="Sulu Archipelago">Sulu</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> The racial make-up of the Christian side was diverse since it were usually made up of <a href="/wiki/Mestizos" class="mw-redirect" title="Mestizos">Mestizos</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mulattoes" class="mw-redirect" title="Mulattoes">Mulattoes</a> and Amerindians (<a href="/wiki/Aztecs" title="Aztecs">Aztecs</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mayans" class="mw-redirect" title="Mayans">Mayans</a> and <a href="/wiki/Incans" class="mw-redirect" title="Incans">Incans</a>) who were gathered and sent from Mexico and were led by Spanish officers who had worked together with native Filipinos in military campaigns across the Southeast Asia.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> The Muslim side though was also equally racially diverse. In addition to the native Malay warriors, the Ottomans had repeatedly sent military expeditions to nearby <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_expedition_to_Aceh" class="mw-redirect" title="Ottoman expedition to Aceh">Aceh</a>. The expeditions were composed mainly of <a href="/wiki/Turkic_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Turkic people">Turks</a>, <a href="/wiki/Egyptians" title="Egyptians">Egyptians</a>, <a href="/wiki/Swahili_people" title="Swahili people">Swahilis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Somalis" title="Somalis">Somalis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sindhis" title="Sindhis">Sindhis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gujaratis" class="mw-redirect" title="Gujaratis">Gujaratis</a> and <a href="/wiki/Malabars" title="Malabars">Malabars</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> These expeditionary forces had also spread to other nearby Sultanates such as Brunei and had taught new fighting tactics and techniques on how to forge cannons.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Eventually, the Spanish invaded the capital on 16 April 1578, with the help of Pengiran Seri Lela and Pengiran Seri Ratna, burning towns and raping populations. The Sultan Saiful Rijal and Paduka Seri Begawan Sultan Abdul Kahar were forced to flee to Meragang then to <a href="/wiki/Jerudong" class="mw-redirect" title="Jerudong">Jerudong</a>. In Jerudong, they made plans to chase the conquering army away from Brunei. Suffering high fatalities due to a <a href="/wiki/Cholera" title="Cholera">cholera</a> or <a href="/wiki/Dysentery" title="Dysentery">dysentery</a> outbreak,<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> the Spanish decided to abandon Brunei and returned to Manila on 26 June 1578, after 72 days. Before doing so, they burned the mosque, a high structure with a five-tier roof.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pengiran Seri Lela died in August or September 1578, probably from the same illness suffered by his Spanish allies.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> There was suspicion that the legitimist sultan could have been poisoned by the ruling sultan.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Seri Lela's daughter, a Bruneian princess, "Putri", had left with the Spanish, she abandoned her claim to the crown and then she married a Christian <a href="/wiki/Tagalog_people" title="Tagalog people">Tagalog</a>, named Agustín de Legazpi de Tondo.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Agustin_de_Legaspi" class="mw-redirect" title="Agustin de Legaspi">Agustin de Legaspi</a> along with his family and associates were soon implicated in the <a href="/wiki/Conspiracy_of_the_Maharlikas" class="mw-redirect" title="Conspiracy of the Maharlikas">Conspiracy of the Maharlikas</a>, an attempt by Filipinos to link up with the Brunei Sultanate and Japanese Shogunate to expel the Spaniards from the Philippines.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-63">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> However, upon the Spanish suppression of the conspiracy, the Bruneian descended aristocracy of precolonial Manila were exiled to <a href="/wiki/Guerrero,_Mexico" class="mw-redirect" title="Guerrero, Mexico">Guerrero, Mexico</a> which consequently later became a center of the <a href="/wiki/Isidoro_Montes_de_Oca" title="Isidoro Montes de Oca">Mexican war of independence against Spain</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-enchistoria_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-enchistoria-64">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-riquezahis_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-riquezahis-65">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The local Brunei accounts<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200941_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200941-66">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> of the Castilian War differ greatly from the generally accepted view of events. What was called the Castilian War was seen as a heroic episode, with the Spaniards being driven out by Bendahara Sakam, purportedly a brother of the ruling sultan, and a thousand native warriors. Most historians consider this to be a <a href="/wiki/Folk-hero" class="mw-redirect" title="Folk-hero">folk-hero</a> account, which probably developed decades or centuries after.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Brunei eventually descended into anarchy. The country suffered a <a href="/wiki/Brunei_Civil_War" title="Brunei Civil War">civil war</a> from 1660 to 1673.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="British_intervention">British intervention</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/British_Brunei_Administrators" class="mw-redirect" title="British Brunei Administrators">British Brunei Administrators</a> and <a href="/wiki/British_Borneo" title="British Borneo">British Borneo</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:The_signing_of_the_Treaty_of_Labuan_on_18_December_1846.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/41/The_signing_of_the_Treaty_of_Labuan_on_18_December_1846.jpg/220px-The_signing_of_the_Treaty_of_Labuan_on_18_December_1846.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/41/The_signing_of_the_Treaty_of_Labuan_on_18_December_1846.jpg/330px-The_signing_of_the_Treaty_of_Labuan_on_18_December_1846.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/41/The_signing_of_the_Treaty_of_Labuan_on_18_December_1846.jpg/440px-The_signing_of_the_Treaty_of_Labuan_on_18_December_1846.jpg 2x" data-file-width="600" data-file-height="405" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:The_signing_of_the_Treaty_of_Labuan_on_18_December_1846.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>British adventurer <a href="/wiki/James_Brooke" title="James Brooke">James Brooke</a> negotiating with the <a href="/wiki/Sultan_of_Brunei" class="mw-redirect" title="Sultan of Brunei">Sultan of Brunei</a>, which led to the signing of the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Labuan" title="Treaty of Labuan">Treaty of Labuan</a>, 1846</div></div></div>
<p>The British have intervened in the affairs of Brunei on several occasions. Britain attacked Brunei in July 1846 due to internal conflicts over who was the rightful Sultan.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200952_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200952-68">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the 1880s, the decline of the <a href="/wiki/Bruneian_Empire" class="mw-redirect" title="Bruneian Empire">Bruneian Empire</a> continued. The sultan granted land (now Sarawak) to <a href="/wiki/James_Brooke" title="James Brooke">James Brooke</a>, who had helped him quell a rebellion, and allowed him to establish the <a href="/wiki/Raj_of_Sarawak" title="Raj of Sarawak">Raj of Sarawak</a>. Over time, Brooke and his nephews (who succeeded him) leased or annexed more land. Brunei lost much of its territory to him and his dynasty, known as the <a href="/wiki/White_Rajahs" title="White Rajahs">White Rajahs</a>.
</p><p>Sultan <a href="/wiki/Hashim_Jalilul_Alam_Aqamaddin" title="Hashim Jalilul Alam Aqamaddin">Hashim Jalilul Alam Aqamaddin</a> appealed to the British to stop further encroachment by the Brookes.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam20093_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam20093-69">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> The "Treaty of Protection" was negotiated by <a href="/wiki/Sir_Hugh_Low" class="mw-redirect" title="Sir Hugh Low">Sir Hugh Low</a> and signed into effect on 17 September 1888. The treaty said that the sultan "could not cede or lease any territory to foreign powers without British consent"; it provided <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom">Britain</a> effective control over Brunei's external affairs, making it a <a href="/wiki/British_protected_state" class="mw-redirect" title="British protected state">British protected state</a> (which continued until 1984).<sup id="cite_ref-cia_43-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> But, when the Raj of Sarawak annexed Brunei's <a href="/wiki/Limbang_Division" title="Limbang Division">Pandaruan District</a> in 1890,<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> the British did not take any action to stop it. They did not regard either Brunei or the Raj of Sarawak as 'foreign' (per the Treaty of Protection). This final annexation by Sarawak left Brunei with its current small land mass and separation into two parts.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200958_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200958-72">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/British_resident" class="mw-redirect" title="British resident">British residents</a> were introduced in Brunei under the Supplementary Protectorate Agreement in 1906.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200959_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200959-73">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> The residents were to advise the sultan on all matters of administration. Over time, the resident assumed more executive control than the sultan. The residential system ended in 1959.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200967_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200967-75">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Discovery_of_oil">Discovery of oil</span></h3>
<p>Petroleum was discovered in 1929 after several fruitless attempts.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200912_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200912-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> Two men, F. F. Marriot and T. G. Cochrane, smelled oil near the <a href="/wiki/Seria" title="Seria">Seria</a> river in late 1926.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200913_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200913-77">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> They informed a <a href="/wiki/Geophysicist" class="mw-redirect" title="Geophysicist">geophysicist</a>, who conducted a survey there. In 1927, gas seepages were reported in the area. Seria Well Number One (S-1) was drilled on 12 July 1928. Oil was struck at 297 metres (974&#160;ft) on 5 April 1929. Seria Well Number 2 was drilled on 19 August 1929, and, as of 2009<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Brunei&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, continues to produce oil.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200914_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200914-78">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> Oil production was increased considerably in the 1930s with the development of more oil fields. In 1940, oil production was at more than six million barrels.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200914_78-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200914-78">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> The British Malayan Petroleum Company (now <a href="/wiki/Brunei_Shell_Petroleum" class="mw-redirect" title="Brunei Shell Petroleum">Brunei Shell Petroleum</a> Company) was formed on 22 July 1922.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200915_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200915-79">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> The first offshore well was drilled in 1957.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> Oil and natural gas have been the basis of Brunei's development and wealth since the late 20th century.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Japanese_occupation">Japanese occupation</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Japanese_occupation_of_British_Borneo" title="Japanese occupation of British Borneo">Japanese occupation of British Borneo</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"/><table class="box-More_citations_needed plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Refimprove" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/75px-Question_book-new.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/100px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>needs additional citations for <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">verification</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Brunei&amp;action=edit">improve this article</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed.<br /><small><span class="plainlinks"><i>Find sources:</i>&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?as_eq=wikipedia&amp;q=%22Brunei%22">"Brunei"</a>&#160;–&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?tbm=nws&amp;q=%22Brunei%22+-wikipedia&amp;tbs=ar:1">news</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?&amp;q=%22Brunei%22&amp;tbs=bkt:s&amp;tbm=bks">newspapers</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?tbs=bks:1&amp;q=%22Brunei%22+-wikipedia">books</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//scholar.google.com/scholar?q=%22Brunei%22">scholar</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.jstor.org/action/doBasicSearch?Query=%22Brunei%22&amp;acc=on&amp;wc=on">JSTOR</a></span></small></span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">December 2017</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Sultan_Ahmad_Tajuddin_(AWM_P10841.001).JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Sultan_Ahmad_Tajuddin_%28AWM_P10841.001%29.JPG/220px-Sultan_Ahmad_Tajuddin_%28AWM_P10841.001%29.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="296" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Sultan_Ahmad_Tajuddin_%28AWM_P10841.001%29.JPG/330px-Sultan_Ahmad_Tajuddin_%28AWM_P10841.001%29.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Sultan_Ahmad_Tajuddin_%28AWM_P10841.001%29.JPG/440px-Sultan_Ahmad_Tajuddin_%28AWM_P10841.001%29.JPG 2x" data-file-width="474" data-file-height="637" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Sultan_Ahmad_Tajuddin_(AWM_P10841.001).JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Ahmad_Tajuddin" title="Ahmad Tajuddin">Ahmad Tajuddin</a>, the <a href="/wiki/List_of_Sultans_of_Brunei" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Sultans of Brunei">27th Sultan of Brunei</a>, with members of his court in April 1941, eight months before the Japanese invaded Brunei</div></div></div>
<p>The Japanese invaded Brunei on 16 December 1941, eight days after their <a href="/wiki/Attack_on_Pearl_Harbor" title="Attack on Pearl Harbor">attack on Pearl Harbor</a> and the United States Navy. They landed 10,000 troops of the <a href="/wiki/Kawaguchi_Detachment" title="Kawaguchi Detachment">Kawaguchi Detachment</a> from <a href="/wiki/Cam_Ranh_Bay" title="Cam Ranh Bay">Cam Ranh Bay</a> at <a href="/wiki/Kuala_Belait" title="Kuala Belait">Kuala Belait</a>. After six days' fighting, they occupied the entire country. The only Allied troops in the area were the 2nd Battalion of the <a href="/wiki/15th_Punjab_Regiment" title="15th Punjab Regiment">15th Punjab Regiment</a> based at <a href="/wiki/Kuching" title="Kuching">Kuching</a>, Sarawak.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Once the Japanese occupied Brunei, they made an agreement with <a href="/wiki/Ahmad_Tajuddin" title="Ahmad Tajuddin">Sultan Ahmad Tajuddin</a> over governing the country. Inche Ibrahim (known later as Pehin Datu Perdana Menteri Dato Laila Utama Awang Haji Ibrahim), a former Secretary to the British Resident, Ernest Edgar Pengilly, was appointed <a href="/wiki/Chief_Administrative_Officer" class="mw-redirect" title="Chief Administrative Officer">Chief Administrative Officer</a> under the Japanese Governor. The Japanese had proposed that Pengilly retain his position under their administration, but he declined. Both he and other British nationals still in Brunei were interned by the Japanese at <a href="/wiki/Batu_Lintang_camp" title="Batu Lintang camp">Batu Lintang camp</a> in Sarawak. While the British officials were under Japanese guard, Ibrahim made a point of personally shaking each one by the hand and wishing him well.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Sultan retained his throne and was given a pension and honours by the Japanese. During the later part of the occupation, he resided at Tantuya, <a href="/wiki/Limbang" title="Limbang">Limbang</a> and had little to do with the Japanese. Most of the Malay government officers were retained by the Japanese. Brunei's administration was reorganised into five prefectures, which included British <a href="/wiki/North_Borneo" title="North Borneo">North Borneo</a>. The Prefectures included <a href="/wiki/Miri_Division" title="Miri Division">Baram</a>, <a href="/wiki/Labuan" title="Labuan">Labuan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lawas" title="Lawas">Lawas</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Limbang" title="Limbang">Limbang</a>. Ibrahim hid numerous significant government documents from the Japanese during the occupation. Pengiran Yusuf (later YAM Pengiran Setia Negara Pengiran <a href="/wiki/Haji_Mohd_Yusuf" class="mw-redirect" title="Haji Mohd Yusuf">Haji Mohd Yusuf</a>), along with other Bruneians, was sent to Japan for training. Although in the area the day of the <a href="/wiki/Atomic_bombings_of_Hiroshima_and_Nagasaki" title="Atomic bombings of Hiroshima and Nagasaki">atomic bombing of Hiroshima</a>, Yusuf survived.
</p><p>The British had anticipated a Japanese attack, but lacked the resources to defend the area because of their engagement in the war in Europe. The troops from the Punjab Regiment filled in the <a href="/wiki/Seria" title="Seria">Seria</a> oilfield oilwells with concrete in September 1941 to deny the Japanese their use. The remaining equipment and installations were destroyed when the <a href="/wiki/Japanese_invasion_of_Malaya" class="mw-redirect" title="Japanese invasion of Malaya">Japanese invaded Malaya</a>. By the end of the war, 16 wells at <a href="/wiki/Miri,_Malaysia" class="mw-redirect" title="Miri, Malaysia">Miri</a> and Seria had been restarted, with production reaching about half the pre-war level. Coal production at <a href="/wiki/Muara,_Brunei" title="Muara, Brunei">Muara</a> was also recommenced, but with little success.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Japanese_Battleship_Nagato_1944.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Japanese_Battleship_Nagato_1944.jpg/220px-Japanese_Battleship_Nagato_1944.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="137" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Japanese_Battleship_Nagato_1944.jpg/330px-Japanese_Battleship_Nagato_1944.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Japanese_Battleship_Nagato_1944.jpg/440px-Japanese_Battleship_Nagato_1944.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3024" data-file-height="1878" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Japanese_Battleship_Nagato_1944.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Japanese_battleship_Nagato" title="Japanese battleship Nagato">Nagato</a>, <a href="/wiki/Japanese_cruiser_Tone_(1937)" title="Japanese cruiser Tone (1937)">Tone</a>, <a href="/wiki/Japanese_battleship_Yamato" title="Japanese battleship Yamato">Yamato</a> and <a href="/wiki/Japanese_battleship_Musashi" title="Japanese battleship Musashi">Musashi</a> in Brunei Bay in October 1944</div></div></div>
<p>During the occupation, the Japanese had their language taught in schools, and Government officers were required to learn Japanese. The local currency was replaced by what was to become known as <a href="/wiki/Banana_money" class="mw-redirect" title="Banana money"><i>duit pisang</i> (banana money)</a>. From 1943 hyper-inflation destroyed the currency's value and, at the end of the war, this currency was worthless. Allied attacks on shipping eventually caused trade to cease. Food and medicine fell into short supply, and the population suffered from famine and disease.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Brunei_International_Airport" title="Brunei International Airport">airport</a> runway was constructed by the Japanese during the occupation, and in 1943 Japanese naval units were based in <a href="/wiki/Brunei_Bay" title="Brunei Bay">Brunei Bay</a> and Labuan. The naval base was destroyed by Allied bombing, but the airport runway survived. The facility was developed as a public airport. In 1944 the Allies began a bombing campaign against the occupying Japanese, which destroyed much of the town and Kuala Belait, but missed <a href="/wiki/Kampong_Ayer" title="Kampong Ayer">Kampong Ayer</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Japanese_Surrender,_Labuan_(AWM_115989).JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Japanese_Surrender%2C_Labuan_%28AWM_115989%29.JPG/220px-Japanese_Surrender%2C_Labuan_%28AWM_115989%29.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="217" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Japanese_Surrender%2C_Labuan_%28AWM_115989%29.JPG/330px-Japanese_Surrender%2C_Labuan_%28AWM_115989%29.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Japanese_Surrender%2C_Labuan_%28AWM_115989%29.JPG/440px-Japanese_Surrender%2C_Labuan_%28AWM_115989%29.JPG 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="632" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Japanese_Surrender,_Labuan_(AWM_115989).JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/George_Wootten" title="George Wootten">Major-General Wootten</a> of the Australian 9th Division with <a href="/wiki/Masao_Baba" title="Masao Baba">Lieutenant-General Masao Baba</a> (signing) of the Japanese 37th Division at the surrender ceremony at Labuan on 10 September 1945</div></div></div>
<p>On 10 June 1945, the <a href="/wiki/Australian_9th_Division" class="mw-redirect" title="Australian 9th Division">Australian 9th Division</a> landed at Muara under <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_North_Borneo" title="Battle of North Borneo">Operation Oboe Six</a> to recapture Borneo from the Japanese. They were supported by American air and naval units. Brunei town was bombed extensively and recaptured after three days of heavy fighting. Many buildings were destroyed, including the Mosque. The Japanese forces in Brunei, Borneo, and Sarawak, under Lieutenant-General Masao Baba, formally surrendered at Labuan on 10 September 1945. The British Military Administration took over from the Japanese and remained until July 1946.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Post-World_War_II">Post-World War II</span></h3>
<p>After <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, a new government was formed in Brunei under the <a href="/wiki/British_Military_Administration_(Borneo)" title="British Military Administration (Borneo)">British Military Administration</a> (BMA). It consisted mainly of Australian officers and servicemen.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200979_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200979-84">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> The administration of Brunei was passed to the Civil Administration on 6 July 1945. The Brunei State Council was also revived that year.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200980_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200980-85">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> The BMA was tasked to revive the Bruneian economy, which was extensively damaged by the Japanese during their occupation. They also had to put out the fires on the wells of Seria, which had been set by the Japanese prior to their defeat.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200980_85-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200980-85">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Before 1941, the Governor of the Straits Settlements, based in Singapore, was responsible for the duties of British High Commissioner for Brunei, <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Sarawak" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Sarawak">Sarawak</a>, and <a href="/wiki/North_Borneo" title="North Borneo">North Borneo</a> (now <a href="/wiki/Sabah" title="Sabah">Sabah</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200981_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200981-86">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> The first <a href="/wiki/British_High_Commissioner" class="mw-redirect" title="British High Commissioner">British High Commissioner</a> for Brunei was the Governor of Sarawak, Sir Charles Ardon Clarke. The Barisan Pemuda ("Youth Movement"; abbreviated as BARIP) was the first political party to be formed in Brunei, on 12 April 1946. The party intended to "preserve the sovereignty of the Sultan and the country, and to defend the rights of the <a href="/wiki/Ethnic_Malays" class="mw-redirect" title="Ethnic Malays">Malays</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-History131_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History131-87">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> BARIP also contributed to the composition of the country's national anthem. The party was dissolved in 1948 due to inactivity.
</p><p>In 1959, a new constitution was written declaring Brunei a self-governing state, while its foreign affairs, security, and defence remained the responsibility of the United Kingdom.<sup id="cite_ref-US_DOS_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_DOS-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> A small <a href="/wiki/Rebellion" title="Rebellion">rebellion</a> erupted against the monarchy in 1962, which was suppressed with help of the UK.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> Known as the <a href="/wiki/Brunei_Revolt" class="mw-redirect" title="Brunei Revolt">Brunei Revolt</a>, the rebellion contributed to the Sultan's decision to opt out of joining the emerging state now called Malaysia under the umbrella of <a href="/wiki/North_Borneo_Federation" title="North Borneo Federation">North Borneo Federation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-US_DOS_88-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_DOS-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>Brunei gained its independence from the United Kingdom on 1 January 1984.<sup id="cite_ref-US_DOS_88-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_DOS-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> The official National Day, which celebrates the country's independence, is held by tradition on 23 February.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Writing_of_the_Constitution">Writing of the Constitution</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Omar_Ali_Saifuddien_III.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Omar_Ali_Saifuddien_III.jpg/220px-Omar_Ali_Saifuddien_III.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="276" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Omar_Ali_Saifuddien_III.jpg/330px-Omar_Ali_Saifuddien_III.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Omar_Ali_Saifuddien_III.jpg/440px-Omar_Ali_Saifuddien_III.jpg 2x" data-file-width="701" data-file-height="880" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Omar_Ali_Saifuddien_III.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Sultan <a href="/wiki/Omar_Ali_Saifuddien_III" title="Omar Ali Saifuddien III">Omar Ali Saifuddien III</a></div></div></div>
<p>In July 1953, Sultan <a href="/wiki/Omar_Ali_Saifuddien_III" title="Omar Ali Saifuddien III">Omar Ali Saifuddien III</a> formed a seven-member committee named <i>Tujuh Serangkai</i>, to determine the citizens' views regarding a written constitution for Brunei. In May 1954, the Sultan, Resident and High Commissioner met to discuss the findings of the committee. They agreed to authorise the drafting of a constitution. In March 1959, Sultan Omar Ali Saifuddien III led a delegation to London to discuss the proposed Constitution.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200998_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200998-91">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> The British delegation was led by Sir <a href="/wiki/Alan_Lennox-Boyd" class="mw-redirect" title="Alan Lennox-Boyd">Alan Lennox-Boyd</a>, Secretary of State for the Colonies. The British Government later accepted the draft constitution.
</p><p>On 29 September 1959, the Constitution Agreement was signed in <a href="/wiki/Bandar_Seri_Begawan" title="Bandar Seri Begawan">Brunei Town</a>. The agreement was signed by Sultan Omar Ali Saifuddien III and Sir Robert Scott, the Commissioner-General for Southeast Asia.
It included the following provisions:<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200959_73-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200959-73">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>The Sultan was made the Supreme Head of State.</li>
<li>Brunei was responsible for its internal administration.</li>
<li>The British Government was responsible for foreign and defence affairs only.</li>
<li>The post of Resident was abolished and replaced by a British High Commissioner.</li></ul>
<p>Five councils were established:<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam2009100_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam2009100-92">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>The Executive Council</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Legislative_Council_of_Brunei" title="Legislative Council of Brunei">The Legislative Council of Brunei</a></li>
<li>The Privy Council</li>
<li>The Council of Succession</li>
<li>The State Religious Council</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="National_development_plans">National development plans</span></h3>
<p>A series of National Development Plans was initiated by the 28th Sultan of Brunei, <a href="/wiki/Omar_Ali_Saifuddien_III" title="Omar Ali Saifuddien III">Omar Ali Saifuddien III</a>.
</p><p>The first was introduced in 1953.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200992_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200992-93">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> A total sum of B$100 million was approved by the Brunei State Council for the plan. E.R. Bevington, from the Colonial Office in <a href="/wiki/Fiji" title="Fiji">Fiji</a>, was appointed to implement it.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam2009129_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam2009129-94">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> A US$14 million Gas Plant was built under the plan. In 1954, survey and exploration work were undertaken by the Brunei Shell Petroleum on both offshore and onshore fields. By 1956, production reached 114,700 <a href="/wiki/Barrels_per_day" class="mw-redirect" title="Barrels per day">bpd</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:THE_BRITISH_ARMY_IN_BRUNEI,_JANUARY_1963.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/THE_BRITISH_ARMY_IN_BRUNEI%2C_JANUARY_1963.jpg/220px-THE_BRITISH_ARMY_IN_BRUNEI%2C_JANUARY_1963.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="221" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/THE_BRITISH_ARMY_IN_BRUNEI%2C_JANUARY_1963.jpg/330px-THE_BRITISH_ARMY_IN_BRUNEI%2C_JANUARY_1963.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/THE_BRITISH_ARMY_IN_BRUNEI%2C_JANUARY_1963.jpg/440px-THE_BRITISH_ARMY_IN_BRUNEI%2C_JANUARY_1963.jpg 2x" data-file-width="795" data-file-height="800" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:THE_BRITISH_ARMY_IN_BRUNEI,_JANUARY_1963.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>British soldiers in the <a href="/wiki/British_protectorate" title="British protectorate">British protectorate</a> of Brunei on guard in the <a href="/wiki/Seria_Field" class="mw-redirect" title="Seria Field">Seria oilfield</a>, January 1963</div></div></div>
<p>The plan also aided the development of public education. By 1958, expenditure on education totalled at $4 million.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam2009129_94-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam2009129-94">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> Communications were improved, as new roads were built and reconstruction at Berakas Airport was completed in 1954.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200921_95-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200921-95">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The second National Development Plan was launched in 1962.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200921_95-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200921-95">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> A major oil and gas field was discovered in 1963. Developments in the oil and gas sector have continued, and oil production has steadily increased since then.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200996_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200996-96">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> The plan also promoted the production of meat and eggs for consumption by citizens. The fishing industry increased its output by 25% throughout the course of the plan. The <a href="/wiki/List_of_Panamax_ports" title="List of Panamax ports">deepwater port</a> at <a href="/wiki/Muara,_Brunei" title="Muara, Brunei">Muara</a> was also constructed during this period. Power requirements were met, and studies were made to provide electricity to rural areas.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200996_96-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_for_Brunei_Darussalam200996-96">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> Efforts were made to eradicate <a href="/wiki/Malaria" title="Malaria">malaria</a>, an endemic disease in the region, with the help of the <a href="/wiki/World_Health_Organization" title="World Health Organization">World Health Organization</a>. Malaria cases were reduced from 300 cases in 1953 to only 66 cases in 1959.<sup id="cite_ref-History129_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History129-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> The death rate was reduced from 20 per thousand in 1947 to 11.3 per thousand in 1953.<sup id="cite_ref-History129_97-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History129-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> Infectious disease has been prevented by public sanitation and improvement of drainage, and the provision of piped pure water to the population.<sup id="cite_ref-History129_97-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History129-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Independence">Independence</span></h3>
<p>On 14 November 1971, Sultan <a href="/wiki/Hassanal_Bolkiah" title="Hassanal Bolkiah">Hassanal Bolkiah</a> left for London to discuss matters regarding the amendments to the 1959 constitution. A new agreement was signed on 23 November 1971 with the British representative being <a href="/wiki/Anthony_Royle,_Baron_Fanshawe_of_Richmond" title="Anthony Royle, Baron Fanshawe of Richmond">Anthony Royle</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Under this agreement, the following terms were agreed upon:
</p>
<ul><li>Brunei was granted full internal self-government</li>
<li>The UK would still be responsible for external affairs and defence.</li>
<li>Brunei and the UK agreed to share the responsibility for security and defence.</li></ul>
<p>This agreement also caused <a href="/wiki/British_Military_Garrison_Brunei" class="mw-redirect" title="British Military Garrison Brunei">Gurkha units</a> to be deployed in Brunei, where they remain up to this day.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Sultan_of_Brunei_with_Janin_Erih.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Sultan_of_Brunei_with_Janin_Erih.jpg/220px-Sultan_of_Brunei_with_Janin_Erih.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="172" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Sultan_of_Brunei_with_Janin_Erih.jpg/330px-Sultan_of_Brunei_with_Janin_Erih.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Sultan_of_Brunei_with_Janin_Erih.jpg/440px-Sultan_of_Brunei_with_Janin_Erih.jpg 2x" data-file-width="563" data-file-height="439" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Sultan_of_Brunei_with_Janin_Erih.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Sultan <a href="/wiki/Hassanal_Bolkiah" title="Hassanal Bolkiah">Hassanal Bolkiah</a> (right) in 2004</div></div></div>
<p>On 7 January 1979, another treaty was signed between Brunei and the <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom">United Kingdom</a>. It was signed with Lord Goronwy-Roberts being the representative of the UK. This agreement granted Brunei to take over international responsibilities as an independent nation. Britain agreed to assist Brunei in diplomatic matters.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> In May 1983, it was announced by the UK that the date of independence of Brunei would be 1 January 1984.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2017)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>On 31 December 1983, a mass gathering was held on main mosques on all four of the districts of the country and at midnight, on 1 January 1984, the Proclamation of Independence was read by Sultan Hassanal Bolkiah. The sultan subsequently assumed the title "<a href="/wiki/Majesty" title="Majesty">His Majesty</a>", rather than the previous "<a href="/wiki/His_Royal_Highness" class="mw-redirect" title="His Royal Highness">His Royal Highness</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> Brunei was <a href="/wiki/Member_states_of_the_United_Nations" title="Member states of the United Nations">admitted to the United Nations</a> on 22 September 1984, becoming the organisation's 159th member.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="21st_century">21st century</span></h3>
<p>In October 2013, Sultan Hassanal Bolkiah announced his intention to impose <a href="/wiki/Penal_Code" class="mw-redirect" title="Penal Code">Penal Code</a> from the <a href="/wiki/Sharia_law" class="mw-redirect" title="Sharia law">Sharia law</a> on the country's Muslims, which make up roughly two thirds of the country's population.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> This would be implemented in three phases, culminating in 2016, and making Brunei the first and only country in East Asia to introduce Sharia law into its penal code, excluding the subnational Indonesian special territory of <a href="/wiki/Aceh" title="Aceh">Aceh</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> The move attracted international criticism,<sup id="cite_ref-MSNBC_2014-05-07_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MSNBC_2014-05-07-104">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> the United Nations expressing "deep concern".<sup id="cite_ref-BBC-27216798_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC-27216798-105">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Brunei" title="Geography of Brunei">Geography of Brunei</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:292px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Brunei_location_map_Topographic.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Brunei_location_map_Topographic.png/290px-Brunei_location_map_Topographic.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="235" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Brunei_location_map_Topographic.png/435px-Brunei_location_map_Topographic.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Brunei_location_map_Topographic.png/580px-Brunei_location_map_Topographic.png 2x" data-file-width="2331" data-file-height="1892" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Brunei_location_map_Topographic.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A topographic and geographic limits map of Brunei</div></div></div>
<p>Brunei is a southeast Asian country consisting of two unconnected parts with a total area of 5,765 square kilometres (2,226&#160;sq&#160;mi) on the island of Borneo. It has 161 kilometres (100&#160;mi) of coastline next to the <a href="/wiki/South_China_Sea" title="South China Sea">South China Sea</a>, and it shares a 381&#160;km (237&#160;mi) <a href="/wiki/Brunei%E2%80%93Malaysia_border" title="Brunei–Malaysia border">border with Malaysia</a>. It has 500 square kilometres (193&#160;sq&#160;mi) of territorial waters, and a 200-nautical-mile (370&#160;km; 230&#160;mi) <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_43-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
