</p><p>About 97% of the population lives in the larger western part (<a href="/wiki/Belait_District" title="Belait District">Belait</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tutong_District" title="Tutong District">Tutong</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Brunei_and_Muara" class="mw-redirect" title="Brunei and Muara">Brunei-Muara</a>), while only about 10,000 people live in the mountainous eastern part (<a href="/wiki/Temburong_District" title="Temburong District">Temburong District</a>). The total population of Brunei is approximately 408,000 as of July 2010<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Brunei&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, of which around 150,000 live in the capital <a href="/wiki/Bandar_Seri_Begawan" title="Bandar Seri Begawan">Bandar Seri Begawan</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> Other major towns are the port town of <a href="/wiki/Muara,_Brunei" title="Muara, Brunei">Muara</a>, the oil-producing town of <a href="/wiki/Seria" title="Seria">Seria</a> and its neighbouring town, <a href="/wiki/Kuala_Belait" title="Kuala Belait">Kuala Belait</a>. In Belait District, the <a href="/wiki/Panaga" title="Panaga">Panaga</a> area is home to large numbers of Europeans expatriates, due to <a href="/wiki/Royal_Dutch_Shell" class="mw-redirect" title="Royal Dutch Shell">Royal Dutch Shell</a> and British Army housing, and several recreational facilities are located there.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Most of Brunei is within the <a href="/wiki/Borneo_lowland_rain_forests" title="Borneo lowland rain forests">Borneo lowland rain forests</a> ecoregion, which covers most of the island. Areas of <a href="/wiki/Borneo_montane_rain_forests" title="Borneo montane rain forests">mountain rain forests</a> are located inland.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_Brunei" class="mw-redirect" title="Climate of Brunei">climate of Brunei</a> is <a href="/wiki/Tropical_climate" title="Tropical climate">tropical</a> equatorial that is a <a href="/wiki/Tropical_rainforest_climate" title="Tropical rainforest climate">tropical rainforest climate</a><sup id="cite_ref-cia_43-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> more subject to the <a href="/wiki/Intertropical_Convergence_Zone" title="Intertropical Convergence Zone">Intertropical Convergence Zone</a> than the <a href="/wiki/Trade_wind" class="mw-redirect" title="Trade wind">trade winds</a> and with no or rare <a href="/wiki/Cyclone" title="Cyclone">cyclones</a>. Brunei is exposed to the risks stemming from climate change along with other <a href="/wiki/ASEAN" title="ASEAN">ASEAN</a> member states.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics_and_government">Politics and government</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Brunei" title="Politics of Brunei">Politics of Brunei</a> and <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Brunei" title="Government of Brunei">Government of Brunei</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Hassanal_Bolkiah.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Hassanal_Bolkiah.jpg/170px-Hassanal_Bolkiah.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Hassanal_Bolkiah.jpg/255px-Hassanal_Bolkiah.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/5/53/Hassanal_Bolkiah.jpg 2x" data-file-width="312" data-file-height="416" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Hassanal_Bolkiah.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Hassanal_Bolkiah" title="Hassanal Bolkiah">Hassanal Bolkiah</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sultan_of_Brunei" class="mw-redirect" title="Sultan of Brunei">Sultan of Brunei</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>Brunei's political system is governed by the constitution and the national tradition of the Malay Islamic Monarchy (<i><a href="/wiki/Melayu_Islam_Beraja" title="Melayu Islam Beraja">Melayu Islam Beraja</a></i>; MIB). The three components of MIB cover Malay culture, Islamic religion, and the political framework under the monarchy.<sup id="cite_ref-bruneipress.com.bn_111-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bruneipress.com.bn-111">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> It has a legal system based on <a href="/wiki/English_common_law" class="mw-redirect" title="English common law">English common law</a>, although Islamic law (<i><a href="/wiki/Shariah" class="mw-redirect" title="Shariah">shariah</a></i>) supersedes this in some cases.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_43-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Brunei <a href="/wiki/Legislative_Council_of_Brunei" title="Legislative Council of Brunei">has a parliament</a> but there are no elections; the last election was held in <a href="/wiki/Brunei_Revolt" class="mw-redirect" title="Brunei Revolt">1962</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Under Brunei's 1959 constitution, His Majesty <a href="/wiki/Hassanal_Bolkiah" title="Hassanal Bolkiah">Hassanal Bolkiah</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> with full executive authority. Since <a href="/wiki/Brunei_Revolt" class="mw-redirect" title="Brunei Revolt">1962</a>, this authority has included emergency powers, which are renewed every two years. Brunei has technically been under <a href="/wiki/Martial_law" title="Martial law">martial law</a> since the <a href="/wiki/Brunei_Revolt" class="mw-redirect" title="Brunei Revolt">Brunei Revolt</a> of 1962.<sup id="cite_ref-US_DOS_88-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_DOS-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Hassanal_Bolkiah" title="Hassanal Bolkiah">Hassanal Bolkiah</a> also serves as the state's prime minister, <a href="/wiki/Finance_minister" title="Finance minister">finance minister</a> and <a href="/wiki/Defence_minister" title="Defence minister">defence minister</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Brunei" title="Foreign relations of Brunei">Foreign relations of Brunei</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pres._Obama_and_HM_Bolkiah_APEC_2015.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e0/Pres._Obama_and_HM_Bolkiah_APEC_2015.jpg/220px-Pres._Obama_and_HM_Bolkiah_APEC_2015.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e0/Pres._Obama_and_HM_Bolkiah_APEC_2015.jpg/330px-Pres._Obama_and_HM_Bolkiah_APEC_2015.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e0/Pres._Obama_and_HM_Bolkiah_APEC_2015.jpg/440px-Pres._Obama_and_HM_Bolkiah_APEC_2015.jpg 2x" data-file-width="654" data-file-height="436" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pres._Obama_and_HM_Bolkiah_APEC_2015.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Brunei's Sultan and Foreign Minister <a href="/wiki/Hassanal_Bolkiah" title="Hassanal Bolkiah">Hassanal Bolkiah</a> meets with U.S. President <a href="/wiki/Barack_Obama" title="Barack Obama">Barack Obama</a>, 18 November 2015</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Keroncong_Kemayoran_di_Perayaan_50_Tahun_Sultan_Hassanal_Bolkiah_Bertakhta_(2c).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Keroncong_Kemayoran_di_Perayaan_50_Tahun_Sultan_Hassanal_Bolkiah_Bertakhta_%282c%29.jpg/220px-Keroncong_Kemayoran_di_Perayaan_50_Tahun_Sultan_Hassanal_Bolkiah_Bertakhta_%282c%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Keroncong_Kemayoran_di_Perayaan_50_Tahun_Sultan_Hassanal_Bolkiah_Bertakhta_%282c%29.jpg/330px-Keroncong_Kemayoran_di_Perayaan_50_Tahun_Sultan_Hassanal_Bolkiah_Bertakhta_%282c%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Keroncong_Kemayoran_di_Perayaan_50_Tahun_Sultan_Hassanal_Bolkiah_Bertakhta_%282c%29.jpg/440px-Keroncong_Kemayoran_di_Perayaan_50_Tahun_Sultan_Hassanal_Bolkiah_Bertakhta_%282c%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="832" data-file-height="624" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Keroncong_Kemayoran_di_Perayaan_50_Tahun_Sultan_Hassanal_Bolkiah_Bertakhta_(2c).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Sultan Hassanal Bolkiah with Indonesian President <a href="/wiki/Joko_Widodo" title="Joko Widodo">Joko Widodo</a>, 6 October 2017</div></div></div>
<p>Until 1979, Brunei's foreign relations were managed by the UK government. After that, they were handled by the Brunei Diplomatic Service. After independence in 1984, this Service was upgraded to ministerial level and is now known as the Ministry of Foreign Affairs.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Officially, Brunei's foreign policy is as follows:<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>Mutual respect of others' territorial sovereignty, integrity and independence;</li>
<li>The maintenance of friendly relations among nations;</li>
<li>Non-interference in the internal affairs of other countries; and</li>
<li>The maintenance and the promotion of peace, security and stability in the region.</li></ul>
<p>With its traditional ties with the United Kingdom, Brunei became the 49th member of the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth</a> immediately on the day of its independence on 1 January 1984.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> As one of its first initiatives toward improved regional relations, Brunei joined <a href="/wiki/ASEAN" title="ASEAN">ASEAN</a> on 7 January 1984, becoming the sixth member. To achieve recognition of its sovereignty and independence, it joined the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> as a full member on 21 September of that same year.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As an Islamic country, Brunei became a full member of the Organisation of the Islamic Conference (now the <a href="/wiki/Organisation_of_Islamic_Cooperation" title="Organisation of Islamic Cooperation">Organisation of Islamic Cooperation</a>) in January 1984 at the Fourth Islamic Summit held in <a href="/wiki/Morocco" title="Morocco">Morocco</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After its accession to the <a href="/wiki/Asia-Pacific_Economic_Cooperation" title="Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation">Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation</a> forum (APEC) in 1989, Brunei hosted the APEC Economic Leaders' Meeting in November 2000 and the ASEAN Regional Forum (ARF) in July 2002.<sup id="cite_ref-APEC_2000_119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-APEC_2000-119">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> Brunei became a founding member of the <a href="/wiki/World_Trade_Organization" title="World Trade Organization">World Trade Organization</a> (WTO) on 1 January 1995,<sup id="cite_ref-WTO_2008_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WTO_2008-120">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> and is a major player in <a href="/wiki/BIMP-EAGA" class="mw-redirect" title="BIMP-EAGA">BIMP-EAGA</a>, which was formed during the Inaugural Ministers' Meeting in <a href="/wiki/Davao_City" title="Davao City">Davao</a>, <a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a>, on 24 March 1994.<sup id="cite_ref-BIMP_1994_121-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BIMP_1994-121">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Vladimir_Putin_with_Hassanal_Bolkiah-1.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e8/Vladimir_Putin_with_Hassanal_Bolkiah-1.jpg/220px-Vladimir_Putin_with_Hassanal_Bolkiah-1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e8/Vladimir_Putin_with_Hassanal_Bolkiah-1.jpg/330px-Vladimir_Putin_with_Hassanal_Bolkiah-1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e8/Vladimir_Putin_with_Hassanal_Bolkiah-1.jpg/440px-Vladimir_Putin_with_Hassanal_Bolkiah-1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="334" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Vladimir_Putin_with_Hassanal_Bolkiah-1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Sultan Hassanal Bolkiah and <a href="/wiki/Vladimir_Putin" title="Vladimir Putin">Vladimir Putin</a> during <a href="/wiki/APEC" class="mw-redirect" title="APEC">APEC 2000</a></div></div></div>
<p>Brunei shares a close relationship with <a href="/wiki/Singapore" title="Singapore">Singapore</a> and the Philippines. In April 2009, Brunei and the Philippines signed a <a href="/wiki/Memorandum_of_Understanding" class="mw-redirect" title="Memorandum of Understanding">Memorandum of Understanding</a> (MOU) that seeks to strengthen the bilateral co-operation of the two countries in the fields of agriculture and farm-related trade and investments.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Brunei is one of many nations to lay claim to some of the disputed <a href="/wiki/Spratly_Islands" title="Spratly Islands">Spratly Islands</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-dispute_123-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dispute-123">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> The status of <a href="/wiki/Limbang" title="Limbang">Limbang</a> as part of Sarawak has been disputed by Brunei since the area was first annexed in 1890.<sup id="cite_ref-dispute_123-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dispute-123">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> The issue was reportedly settled in 2009, with Brunei agreeing to accept the border in exchange for Malaysia giving up claims to oil fields in Bruneian waters.<sup id="cite_ref-bt_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bt-124">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> The Brunei government denies this and says that their claim on Limbang was never dropped.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Brunei was the chair for <a href="/wiki/ASEAN" title="ASEAN">ASEAN</a> in 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> It also hosted the ASEAN summit on that same year.<sup id="cite_ref-RA_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RA-128">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Defence">Defence</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Royal_Brunei_Armed_Forces" title="Royal Brunei Armed Forces">Royal Brunei Armed Forces</a> and <a href="/wiki/British_Military_Garrison_Brunei" class="mw-redirect" title="British Military Garrison Brunei">British Military Garrison Brunei</a></div>
<p>Brunei maintains three infantry battalions stationed around the country.<sup id="cite_ref-US_DOS_88-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_DOS-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Royal_Brunei_Navy" title="Royal Brunei Navy">Brunei navy</a> has several "Ijtihad"-class patrol boats purchased from a German manufacturer. The United Kingdom also maintains a base in <a href="/wiki/Seria" title="Seria">Seria</a>, the centre of the oil industry in Brunei. A <a href="/wiki/Gurkha" title="Gurkha">Gurkha</a> battalion consisting of 1,500 personnel is stationed there.<sup id="cite_ref-US_DOS_88-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_DOS-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> United Kingdom military personnel are stationed there under a defence agreement signed between the two countries.<sup id="cite_ref-US_DOS_88-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_DOS-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A <a href="/wiki/Bell_212" title="Bell 212">Bell 212</a> operated by the air force crashed in <a href="/wiki/Kuala_Belait" title="Kuala Belait">Kuala Belait</a> on 20 July 2012 with the loss of 12 of the 14 crew on board. The cause of the accident has yet to be ascertained.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> The crash is the worst aviation incident in the history of Brunei.
</p><p>The Army is currently acquiring new equipment,<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> including <a href="/wiki/UAV" class="mw-redirect" title="UAV">UAVs</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sikorsky_S-70" title="Sikorsky S-70">S-70i Black Hawks</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Brunei's Legislative Council proposed an increase of the defence budget for the 2016–17 fiscal year of about five percent to 564 million Brunei dollars ($408 million). This amounts to about ten per cent of the state's total national yearly expenditure and represents around 2.5 per cent of GDP.<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Brunei" title="Administrative divisions of Brunei">Administrative divisions of Brunei</a></div>
<p>Brunei is divided into four <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Brunei" title="Districts of Brunei">districts</a> (<span title="Malay (macrolanguage)-language text"><i lang="ms">daerah</i></span>), namely <a href="/wiki/Brunei-Muara_District" title="Brunei-Muara District">Brunei-Muara</a>, <a href="/wiki/Belait_District" title="Belait District">Belait</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tutong_District" title="Tutong District">Tutong</a> and <a href="/wiki/Temburong_District" title="Temburong District">Temburong</a>. Brunei-Muara District is the smallest yet the most populous, and home to the country's capital Bandar Seri Begawan. Belait is the birthplace and centre for the country's oil and gas industry. Temburong is an <a href="/wiki/Exclave" class="mw-redirect" title="Exclave">exclave</a> and separated from the rest of the country by the <a href="/wiki/Brunei_Bay" title="Brunei Bay">Brunei Bay</a> and <a href="/wiki/Malaysia" title="Malaysia">Malaysian</a> <a href="/wiki/States_of_Malaysia" class="mw-redirect" title="States of Malaysia">state</a> of <a href="/wiki/Sarawak" title="Sarawak">Sarawak</a>. Tutong is home to <a href="/wiki/Tasek_Merimbun" title="Tasek Merimbun">Tasek Merimbun</a>, the country's largest natural lake.
</p><p>Each district is divided into several <a href="/wiki/Mukims_of_Brunei" title="Mukims of Brunei">mukims</a>. Altogether there are 39 mukims in Brunei. Each mukim encompasses several <a href="/wiki/Villages_of_Brunei" title="Villages of Brunei">villages</a> (<span title="Malay (macrolanguage)-language text"><i lang="ms">kampung</i></span> or <span title="Malay (macrolanguage)-language text"><i lang="ms">kampong</i></span>).
</p><p>Bandar Seri Begawan and towns in the country (except <a href="/wiki/Pekan_Muara" class="mw-redirect" title="Pekan Muara">Muara</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bangar,_Brunei" title="Bangar, Brunei">Bangar</a>) are administered as <a href="/wiki/Municipalities_of_Brunei" title="Municipalities of Brunei">Municipal Board areas</a> (<span title="Malay (macrolanguage)-language text"><i lang="ms">kawasan Lembaga Bandaran</i></span>). Each municipal area may constitute villages or mukims, partially or as a whole. Bandar Seri Begawan and a few of the towns also function as capitals of the districts where they are located.
</p><p>A district and its constituent mukims and villages are administered by a District Office (<span title="Malay (macrolanguage)-language text"><i lang="ms">Jabatan Daerah</i></span>). Meanwhile, municipal areas are governed by Municipal Departments (<span title="Malay (macrolanguage)-language text"><i lang="ms">Jabatan Bandaran</i></span>). Both District Offices and Municipal Departments are government departments under the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Home_Affairs_(Brunei)" title="Ministry of Home Affairs (Brunei)">Ministry of Home Affairs</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legal_system">Legal system</span></h2>
<p>Brunei has numerous courts in its judicial branch. The highest court, though subject in civil cases to the appellate jurisdiction of the <a href="/wiki/Judicial_Committee_of_the_Privy_Council" title="Judicial Committee of the Privy Council">Judicial Committee of the Privy Council</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>  is the Supreme Court, which consists of the Court of Appeal and High Court. Both of these have a chief justice and two judges.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_43-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Women_and_children">Women and children</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Women_in_Brunei" title="Women in Brunei">Women in Brunei</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:US_Navy_111004-N-VP123-071_Lt._j.g._Artemas_Richardson,_a_public_affairs_officer_aboard_the_Arleigh_Burke-class_guided-missile_destroyer_USS_Dewey.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/US_Navy_111004-N-VP123-071_Lt._j.g._Artemas_Richardson%2C_a_public_affairs_officer_aboard_the_Arleigh_Burke-class_guided-missile_destroyer_USS_Dewey.jpg/220px-US_Navy_111004-N-VP123-071_Lt._j.g._Artemas_Richardson%2C_a_public_affairs_officer_aboard_the_Arleigh_Burke-class_guided-missile_destroyer_USS_Dewey.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="157" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/US_Navy_111004-N-VP123-071_Lt._j.g._Artemas_Richardson%2C_a_public_affairs_officer_aboard_the_Arleigh_Burke-class_guided-missile_destroyer_USS_Dewey.jpg/330px-US_Navy_111004-N-VP123-071_Lt._j.g._Artemas_Richardson%2C_a_public_affairs_officer_aboard_the_Arleigh_Burke-class_guided-missile_destroyer_USS_Dewey.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/US_Navy_111004-N-VP123-071_Lt._j.g._Artemas_Richardson%2C_a_public_affairs_officer_aboard_the_Arleigh_Burke-class_guided-missile_destroyer_USS_Dewey.jpg/440px-US_Navy_111004-N-VP123-071_Lt._j.g._Artemas_Richardson%2C_a_public_affairs_officer_aboard_the_Arleigh_Burke-class_guided-missile_destroyer_USS_Dewey.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2100" data-file-height="1500" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:US_Navy_111004-N-VP123-071_Lt._j.g._Artemas_Richardson,_a_public_affairs_officer_aboard_the_Arleigh_Burke-class_guided-missile_destroyer_USS_Dewey.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Headscarves called <i><a href="/wiki/Tudong" title="Tudong">tudong</a></i> are compulsory for Brunei's Muslim schoolgirls</div></div></div>
<p>The U.S. Department of State has stated that discrimination against women is a problem in Brunei.<sup id="cite_ref-state.gov_134-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state.gov-134">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> The law prohibits sexual harassment and stipulates that whoever assaults or uses criminal force, intending thereby to outrage or knowing it is likely to outrage the modesty of a person, shall be punished with imprisonment for as much as five years and <a href="/wiki/Caning" title="Caning">caning</a>. The law stipulates imprisonment of up to 30 years, and caning with not fewer than 12 strokes for rape. The law does not criminalise <a href="/wiki/Marital_rape" title="Marital rape">spousal rape</a>; it explicitly states that sexual intercourse by a man with his wife, as long as she is not under 13 years of age, is not rape. Protections against sexual assault by a spouse are provided under the amended Islamic Family Law Order 2010 and Married Women Act Order 2010. The penalty for breaching a protection order is a fine not exceeding BN$2,000 or imprisonment not exceeding six months. By law, sexual intercourse with a female under 14 years of age constitutes rape and is punishable by imprisonment for not less than eight years and not more than 30 years and not less than 12 strokes of the cane. The intent of the law is to protect girls from exploitation through prostitution and "other immoral purposes", including pornography.<sup id="cite_ref-state.gov_134-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state.gov-134">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Bruneian_nationality_law" title="Bruneian nationality law">Bruneian citizenship</a> is derived through parents' nationality rather than <i><a href="/wiki/Jus_soli" title="Jus soli">jus soli</a></i>. Parents with stateless status are required to apply for a special pass for a child born in the country. Failure to register a child may make it difficult to enroll the child in school.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="LGBT_rights">LGBT rights</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Brunei" title="LGBT rights in Brunei">LGBT rights in Brunei</a></div>
<p>Male and female <a href="/wiki/Homosexuality" title="Homosexuality">homosexuality</a> is <a href="/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Brunei" title="LGBT rights in Brunei">illegal in Brunei</a>. Sexual relations between men are punishable by death or whipping; sex between women is punishable by caning or imprisonment.
</p><p>In May 2019, the Brunei government extended its existing moratorium on the death penalty to the Sharia criminal code as well that made homosexual acts punishable with death by stoning.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2019, Brunei announced that it would no longer be implementing the second phase of its controversial sharia penal code. The code, which was first introduced in 2014, included a range of punishments for crimes such as theft, drug offences, and same-sex relationships, including amputation and death by stoning.
</p><p>The decision to halt the implementation of the second phase of the code came after significant international backlash and pressure from countries and human rights organizations, who criticized the harsh punishments as inhumane and a violation of human rights.
</p><p>The government of Brunei stated that the decision was made in order to maintain peace and stability in the country, and to avoid any negative impact on the economy and reputation of the country. The Sultan of Brunei, Hassanal Bolkiah, also issued a statement saying that the country would continue to "strengthen and improve" its legal system in line with international norms and best practices.
</p><p>It is worth mentioning that the first phase of the sharia penal code, which includes fines and imprisonment for offenses such as failure to attend Friday prayers and consuming alcohol, remains in place.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religious_rights">Religious rights</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_religion_in_Brunei" title="Freedom of religion in Brunei">Freedom of religion in Brunei</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Brunei_International_Airport_Surau.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Brunei_International_Airport_Surau.jpg/220px-Brunei_International_Airport_Surau.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Brunei_International_Airport_Surau.jpg/330px-Brunei_International_Airport_Surau.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Brunei_International_Airport_Surau.jpg/440px-Brunei_International_Airport_Surau.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4128" data-file-height="3096" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Brunei_International_Airport_Surau.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Brunei International Airport Mosque</div></div></div>
<p>In The Laws of Brunei, the right of non-Muslims to practice their faith is guaranteed by the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Brunei" title="Constitution of Brunei">1959 Constitution</a>. However, celebrations and prayers must be confined to places of worship and private residences.<sup id="cite_ref-m.bt.com.bn_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-m.bt.com.bn-136">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> Upon adopting Sharia Penal Code, the Ministry of Religious Affairs banned <a href="/wiki/Christmas" title="Christmas">Christmas</a> decorations in public places, but did not forbid celebration of Christmas in places of worship and private premises.<sup id="cite_ref-Brunei_unfairly_hurt_137-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Brunei_unfairly_hurt-137">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>
On 25 December 2015, 4,000 out of 18,000 estimated local Catholics attended the mass of Christmas Day and Christmas Eve.<sup id="cite_ref-m.bt.com.bn_136-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-m.bt.com.bn-136">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> In 2015, the then-<a href="/wiki/Cornelius_Sim" title="Cornelius Sim">head</a> of the Catholic Church in Brunei told <i><a href="/wiki/The_Brunei_Times" title="The Brunei Times">The Brunei Times</a></i>, "To be quite honest there has been no change for us this year; no new restrictions have been laid down, although we fully respect and adhere to the existing regulations that our celebrations and worship be [confined] to the compounds of the church and private residences".<sup id="cite_ref-m.bt.com.bn_136-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-m.bt.com.bn-136">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Brunei's revised penal code came into force in phases, commencing on 22 April 2014 with offences punishable by fines or imprisonment.<sup id="cite_ref-Brunei140430_138-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Brunei140430-138">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-DW-140430_139-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DW-140430-139">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> The complete code, due for final implementation later, stipulated the <a href="/wiki/Death_penalty" class="mw-redirect" title="Death penalty">death penalty</a> for numerous offenses (both violent and non-violent), such as insult or defamation of <a href="/wiki/Muhammad" title="Muhammad">Muhammad</a>, insulting any verses of the Quran and Hadith, <a href="/wiki/Blasphemy" title="Blasphemy">blasphemy</a>, declaring oneself a prophet or non-Muslim, robbery, rape, adultery, sodomy, extramarital sexual relations for Muslims, and murder. Stoning to death was the specified "method of execution for crimes of a sexual nature". Rupert Colville, spokesperson for the <a href="/wiki/Office_of_the_UN_High_Commissioner_for_Human_Rights" class="mw-redirect" title="Office of the UN High Commissioner for Human Rights">Office of the UN High Commissioner for Human Rights</a> (OHCHR) declared that, "Application of the death penalty for such a broad range of offences contravenes international law."<sup id="cite_ref-UN_2014_140-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_2014-140">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Animal_rights">Animal rights</span></h3>
<p>Brunei is the first country in Asia to have banned <a href="/wiki/Shark_finning" title="Shark finning">shark finning</a> nationwide.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Brunei has retained most of its forests, compared to its neighbours that share <a href="/wiki/Borneo" title="Borneo">Borneo</a> island. There is a public campaign calling to protect <a href="/wiki/Pangolin" title="Pangolin">pangolins</a> which are considered a threatened treasure in Brunei.<sup id="cite_ref-Hii2015_142-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hii2015-142">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Brunei" title="Economy of Brunei">Economy of Brunei</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Brunei" title="Tourism in Brunei">Tourism in Brunei</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:292px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Brunei_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Brunei_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/290px-Brunei_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="189" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Brunei_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/435px-Brunei_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Brunei_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/580px-Brunei_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Brunei_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Brunei exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:SBY_dan_para_pemimpin_ASEAN_25-04-2013.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/SBY_dan_para_pemimpin_ASEAN_25-04-2013.jpg/220px-SBY_dan_para_pemimpin_ASEAN_25-04-2013.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="168" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/SBY_dan_para_pemimpin_ASEAN_25-04-2013.jpg/330px-SBY_dan_para_pemimpin_ASEAN_25-04-2013.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/SBY_dan_para_pemimpin_ASEAN_25-04-2013.jpg/440px-SBY_dan_para_pemimpin_ASEAN_25-04-2013.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1500" data-file-height="1144" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:SBY_dan_para_pemimpin_ASEAN_25-04-2013.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/BIMP-EAGA" class="mw-redirect" title="BIMP-EAGA">BIMP-EAGA</a> meeting in the office of Brunei Prime Minister on 25 April 2013. From left: Brunei Sultan Hassanal Bolkiah, Indonesian President <a href="/wiki/Susilo_Bambang_Yudhoyono" title="Susilo Bambang Yudhoyono">Susilo Bambang Yudhoyono</a>, Malaysian Representative and Filipino President <a href="/wiki/Benigno_Aquino_III" title="Benigno Aquino III">Benigno Aquino III</a>. Brunei is part of the BIMP-EAGA, a subregional economic co-operation initiative in Southeast Asia.</div></div></div>
<p>Brunei has the second-highest <a href="/wiki/Human_Development_Index" title="Human Development Index">Human Development Index</a> among the <a href="/wiki/Southeast_Asian" class="mw-redirect" title="Southeast Asian">Southeast Asian</a> nations, after <a href="/wiki/Singapore" title="Singapore">Singapore</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Petroleum" title="Petroleum">Crude oil</a> and natural gas production account for about 90% of its GDP.<sup id="cite_ref-US_DOS_88-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_DOS-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> About 167,000 barrels (26,600&#160;m<sup>3</sup>) of oil are produced every day, making Brunei the fourth-largest producer of oil in Southeast Asia.<sup id="cite_ref-US_DOS_88-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_DOS-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> It also produces approximately 25.3&#160;million cubic metres (890<span style="margin-left:0.2em">×<span style="margin-left:0.1em">10</span></span><s style="display:none">^</s><sup>6</sup>&#160;cu&#160;ft) of <a href="/wiki/Liquified_natural_gas" class="mw-redirect" title="Liquified natural gas">liquified natural gas</a> per day, making Brunei the ninth-largest gas exporter in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-US_DOS_88-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_DOS-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> <i><a href="/wiki/Forbes" title="Forbes">Forbes</a></i> also ranks Brunei as the fifth-richest nation out of 182, based on its petroleum and natural gas fields.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Substantial income from overseas investment supplements income from domestic production. Most of these investments are made by the Brunei Investment Agency, an arm of the Ministry of Finance.<sup id="cite_ref-US_DOS_88-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_DOS-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> The government provides for all medical services,<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> and subsidises rice<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> and housing.<sup id="cite_ref-US_DOS_88-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_DOS-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The national <a href="/wiki/Flag_carrier" title="Flag carrier">air carrier</a>, <a href="/wiki/Royal_Brunei_Airlines" title="Royal Brunei Airlines">Royal Brunei Airlines</a>, is trying to develop Brunei as a hub for international travel between Europe and Australia/New Zealand. Central to this strategy is the position that the airline maintains at <a href="/wiki/London_Heathrow_Airport" class="mw-redirect" title="London Heathrow Airport">London Heathrow Airport</a>. It holds a daily slot at the highly capacity-controlled airport, which it serves from Bandar Seri Begawan via Dubai. The airline also has services to major Asian destinations including Shanghai, Bangkok, Singapore and Manila.
</p><p>Brunei depends heavily on imports such as agricultural products (e.g. rice, <a href="/wiki/Convenience_food" title="Convenience food">food products</a>, livestock, etc.),<sup id="cite_ref-agriculture_brunei_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-agriculture_brunei-148">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> vehicles and <a href="/wiki/Consumer_electronics" title="Consumer electronics">electrical products</a> from other countries.<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> Brunei imports 60% of its food; of that amount, around 75% come from other ASEAN countries.<sup id="cite_ref-agriculture_brunei_148-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-agriculture_brunei-148">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Brunei's leaders are concerned that increasing integration in the world economy will undermine internal <a href="/wiki/Social_cohesion" class="mw-redirect" title="Social cohesion">social cohesion</a> and have therefore pursued an isolationist policy. However, it has become a more prominent player by serving as chairman for the 2000 <a href="/wiki/Asia-Pacific_Economic_Cooperation" title="Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation">Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation</a> (APEC) forum. Brunei's leaders plan to upgrade the <a href="/wiki/Labour_(economics)" class="mw-redirect" title="Labour (economics)">labour</a> force, reduce unemployment, which was at 6.9% in 2014;<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> strengthen the banking and tourism sectors, and, in general, broaden the economic base.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> A long-term development plan aims to diversify growth.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The government of Brunei has also promoted food self-sufficiency, especially in <a href="/wiki/Rice" title="Rice">rice</a>. Brunei renamed its Brunei Darussalam Rice 1 as Laila Rice during the launch of the "Padi Planting Towards Achieving Self-Sufficiency of Rice Production in Brunei Darussalam" ceremony at the Wasan padi fields in April 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> In August 2009, the Royal Family reaped the first few Laila padi stalks, after years of attempts to boost local rice production, a goal first articulated about half a century ago.<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> In July 2009 Brunei launched its national <a href="/wiki/Halal" title="Halal">halal</a> branding scheme, Brunei Halal, with a goal to export to foreign markets.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2020, Brunei's electricity production was largely based on fossil fuels; renewable energy accounted for less than 1% of produced electricity in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Infrastructure">Infrastructure</span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:V8-DLA_Boeing_787_of_Royal_Brunei_at_Heathrow_(11400528073).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/V8-DLA_Boeing_787_of_Royal_Brunei_at_Heathrow_%2811400528073%29.jpg/220px-V8-DLA_Boeing_787_of_Royal_Brunei_at_Heathrow_%2811400528073%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="126" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/V8-DLA_Boeing_787_of_Royal_Brunei_at_Heathrow_%2811400528073%29.jpg/330px-V8-DLA_Boeing_787_of_Royal_Brunei_at_Heathrow_%2811400528073%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/V8-DLA_Boeing_787_of_Royal_Brunei_at_Heathrow_%2811400528073%29.jpg/440px-V8-DLA_Boeing_787_of_Royal_Brunei_at_Heathrow_%2811400528073%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2101" data-file-height="1200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:V8-DLA_Boeing_787_of_Royal_Brunei_at_Heathrow_(11400528073).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Royal Brunei <a href="/wiki/Boeing_787_Dreamliner" title="Boeing 787 Dreamliner">Boeing 787 Dreamliner</a> at London <a href="/wiki/Heathrow_Airport_Holdings" title="Heathrow Airport Holdings">Heathrow Airport</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>As of 2019, the country's road network constituted a total length of 3,713.57 kilometres (2,307.51&#160;mi), out of which <span data-sort-value="7001867946477378910♠" style="display:none"></span>86.8% were <a href="/wiki/Paved_road" class="mw-redirect" title="Paved road">paved</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-statistics_2019_157-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-statistics_2019-157">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup> The 135-kilometre (84&#160;mi) highway from <a href="/wiki/Muara_Town" class="mw-redirect" title="Muara Town">Muara Town</a> to <a href="/wiki/Kuala_Belait" title="Kuala Belait">Kuala Belait</a> is a <a href="/wiki/Dual_carriageway" title="Dual carriageway">dual carriageway</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-bruneipress.com.bn_111-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bruneipress.com.bn-111">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Brunei is accessible by air, sea, and land transport. <a href="/wiki/Brunei_International_Airport" title="Brunei International Airport">Brunei International Airport</a> is the main entry point to the country. <a href="/wiki/Royal_Brunei_Airlines" title="Royal Brunei Airlines">Royal Brunei Airlines</a><sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> is the national carrier. There is another airfield, the <a href="/wiki/Anduki_Airfield" title="Anduki Airfield">Anduki Airfield</a>, located in <a href="/wiki/Seria" title="Seria">Seria</a>. The ferry terminal at Muara services regular connections to <a href="/wiki/Labuan" title="Labuan">Labuan</a> (Malaysia). Speedboats provide passenger and goods transportation to the Temburong district.<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> The main highway running across Brunei is the Tutong-Muara Highway. The country's road network is well developed. Brunei has one main sea port located at Muara.<sup id="cite_ref-US_DOS_88-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_DOS-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The airport in Brunei is currently being extensively upgraded.<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> Changi Airport International is the consultant working on this modernisation, which planned cost is currently $150 million.<sup id="cite_ref-BT_null_161-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BT_null-161">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Aviation_162-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Aviation-162">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> This project is slated to add 14,000 square metres (150,000&#160;sq&#160;ft) of new floorspace and includes a new terminal and arrival hall.<sup id="cite_ref-BT22_163-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BT22-163">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> With the completion of this project, the annual passenger capacity of the airport is expected to double from 1.5 to 3 million.<sup id="cite_ref-BT_null_161-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BT_null-161">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>With one private car for every 2.09 persons, Brunei has one of the highest car ownership rates in the world. This has been attributed to the absence of a comprehensive transport system, low import tax, and low unleaded petrol price of B$0.53 per litre.<sup id="cite_ref-bruneipress.com.bn_111-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bruneipress.com.bn-111">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A new 30-kilometre (19&#160;mi) roadway <a href="/wiki/Temburong_Bridge" class="mw-redirect" title="Temburong Bridge">connecting the Muara and Temburong</a> districts of Brunei is slated to be completed in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> Fourteen kilometres (9&#160;mi) of this roadway would be crossing the <a href="/wiki/Brunei_Bay" title="Brunei Bay">Brunei Bay</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> The bridge cost is $1.6 billion.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Banking">Banking</span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Bank_of_China" title="Bank of China">Bank of China</a> received permission to open a branch in Brunei in April 2016. <a href="/wiki/Citibank" title="Citibank">Citibank</a>, which entered in 1972, closed its operations in Brunei in 2014. <a href="/wiki/HSBC" title="HSBC">HSBC</a>, which had entered in 1947, closed its operation in Brunei in November 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-HSBCClose_167-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HSBCClose-167">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> May Bank of Malaysia, RHB Bank of Malaysia, Standard Chartered Bank of United Kingdom, United Overseas Bank of Singapore and Bank of China are currently operating in Brunei.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Brunei" title="Demographics of Brunei">Demographics of Brunei</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Sultan_Omar_Ali_Saifuddin_Mosque_02.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Sultan_Omar_Ali_Saifuddin_Mosque_02.jpg/220px-Sultan_Omar_Ali_Saifuddin_Mosque_02.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Sultan_Omar_Ali_Saifuddin_Mosque_02.jpg/330px-Sultan_Omar_Ali_Saifuddin_Mosque_02.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Sultan_Omar_Ali_Saifuddin_Mosque_02.jpg/440px-Sultan_Omar_Ali_Saifuddin_Mosque_02.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3933" data-file-height="2622" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Sultan_Omar_Ali_Saifuddin_Mosque_02.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Sultan_Omar_Ali_Saifuddin_Mosque" class="mw-redirect" title="Sultan Omar Ali Saifuddin Mosque">Sultan Omar Ali Saifuddin Mosque</a> at night.</div></div></div>
<p>Ethnicities indigenous to Brunei include the <a href="/wiki/Belait_people" title="Belait people">Belait</a>, <a href="/wiki/Brunei_Bisaya_language" title="Brunei Bisaya language">Brunei Bisaya</a> (not to be confused with the Bisaya/Visaya of the nearby Philippines), indigenous <a href="/wiki/Bruneian_Malay_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Bruneian Malay people">Bruneian Malay</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dusun_people" title="Dusun people">Dusun</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kedayan" title="Kedayan">Kedayan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lun_Bawang" title="Lun Bawang">Lun Bawang</a>, <a href="/wiki/Murut_people" title="Murut people">Murut</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tutong_language" title="Tutong language">Tutong</a>.
</p><p>The population of Brunei in 2021 was 445,373,<sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_168-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP-168">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_2022_169-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP_2022-169">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> of which 76% live in urban areas. The rate of urbanisation is estimated at 2.13% per year from 2010 to 2015. The average life expectancy is 77.7 years.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> In 2014, 65.7% of the population were <a href="/wiki/Bruneian_Malay_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Bruneian Malay people">Malay</a>, 10.3% are <a href="/wiki/Ethnic_Chinese_in_Brunei" title="Ethnic Chinese in Brunei">Chinese</a>, 3.4% are indigenous, with 20.6% smaller groups making up the rest.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> There is a relatively large <a href="/wiki/Expatriate" title="Expatriate">expatriate</a> community.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup>
Most expats come from non-<a href="/wiki/Muslim" class="mw-redirect" title="Muslim">Muslim</a> countries such as Australia, United Kingdom, South Korea, Japan, The Philippines, Thailand, Cambodia, Vietnam and India.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Brunei" title="Religion in Brunei">Religion in Brunei</a></div>
<div class="PieChartTemplate thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px">
<div class="mw-no-invert" style="background-color:white;margin:auto;position:relative;width:200px;height:200px;overflow:hidden;border-radius:100px;border:1px solid black;transform:scaleX(-1)rotate(-90deg)">
<div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:0; top:0; border-width:0 200px 200px 0; border-color:grey"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 307.76835371753px; border-left-color:blue"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:blue"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:blue"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 105.1572499879px; border-left-color:gold"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:gold"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:gold"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 38.867781852942px; border-left-color:green"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:green"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:green"></div>
</div>
<div class="thumbcaption">
<p>Religion in Brunei<sup id="cite_ref-deps.gov.bn_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-deps.gov.bn-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r981673959">.mw-parser-output .legend{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .legend-color{display:inline-block;min-width:1.25em;height:1.25em;line-height:1.25;margin:1px 0;text-align:center;border:1px solid black;background-color:transparent;color:black}.mw-parser-output .legend-text{}</style><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:green; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a> 337,391 (80.9%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:gold; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism">Buddhism</a> 29,495 (7%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:blue; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a> 29,510 (7.1%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:grey; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Others: indigenous beliefs and non-religious, Atheist or <a href="/wiki/Agnostic" class="mw-redirect" title="Agnostic">Agnostic</a> 20,860 (5%)</div>
</div>
</div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Islam_in_Brunei" title="Islam in Brunei">Islam</a> is the official religion of Brunei,<sup id="cite_ref-cia_43-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> specifically that of the <a href="/wiki/Sunni_Islam" title="Sunni Islam">Sunni</a> denomination and the <a href="/wiki/Shafi%27i" class="mw-redirect" title="Shafi&#39;i">Shafi'i</a> school of Islamic jurisprudence. More than 80% of the population, including the majority of <a href="/wiki/Bruneian_Malay_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Bruneian Malay people">Bruneian Malays</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kedayan" title="Kedayan">Kedayans</a> identify as <a href="/wiki/Muslim" class="mw-redirect" title="Muslim">Muslim</a>. Other faiths practised are <a href="/wiki/Buddhism_in_Brunei" title="Buddhism in Brunei">Buddhism</a> (7%, mainly by the Chinese) and <a href="/wiki/Christianity_in_Brunei" title="Christianity in Brunei">Christianity</a> (7.1%).<sup id="cite_ref-deps.gov.bn_5-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-deps.gov.bn-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Freethought" title="Freethought">Freethinkers</a>, mostly Chinese, form about 7% of the population. Although most of them practise some form of religion with elements of Buddhism, <a href="/wiki/Confucianism" title="Confucianism">Confucianism</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Taoism" title="Taoism">Taoism</a>, they prefer to present themselves as having practised no religion officially, hence labelled as <a href="/wiki/Atheism" title="Atheism">atheists</a> in official censuses. Followers of indigenous religions are about 2% of the population.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Brunei" title="Languages of Brunei">Languages of Brunei</a></div>
<p>The official <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Brunei" title="Languages of Brunei">language of Brunei</a> is <a href="/wiki/Standard_Malay" class="mw-redirect" title="Standard Malay">Standard Malay</a>, for which both the <a href="/wiki/Latin_alphabet" title="Latin alphabet">Latin alphabet</a> (<a href="/wiki/Rumi_script" class="mw-redirect" title="Rumi script">Rumi</a>) and the <a href="/wiki/Arabic_alphabet" title="Arabic alphabet">Arabic alphabet</a> (<a href="/wiki/Jawi_alphabet" class="mw-redirect" title="Jawi alphabet">Jawi</a>) are used.<sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> Initially, Malay was written in <a href="/wiki/Jawi_script" title="Jawi script">Jawi script</a> before it was switched in to Latin alphabet around 1941.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEde_Vienne2016104_175-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEde_Vienne2016104-175">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The principal spoken language is <i>Melayu Brunei</i> (<a href="/wiki/Brunei_Malay" title="Brunei Malay">Brunei Malay</a>). Brunei Malay is rather divergent from standard Malay and the rest of the Malay dialects, being about 84% cognate with standard Malay,<sup id="cite_ref-Martin_176-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Martin-176">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> and is mostly mutually intelligible with it.<sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/English_language" title="English language">English</a> is widely used as a business and official language and it is spoken by a majority of the population in Brunei. English is used in business as a <a href="/wiki/Working_language" title="Working language">working language</a> and as the language of instruction from primary to tertiary education.<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Chinese languages are also widely spoken, and the <a href="/wiki/Ethnic_Chinese_in_Brunei" title="Ethnic Chinese in Brunei">Chinese minority</a> in Brunei speak a number of <a href="/wiki/Varieties_of_Chinese" title="Varieties of Chinese">varieties of Chinese</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Arabic" title="Arabic">Arabic</a> is the religious language of Muslims and is taught in schools, particularly religious schools, and also in institutes of higher learning. As of 2004, there are six Arabic schools and one religious teachers' college in Brunei. A majority of Brunei's Muslim population has had some form of formal or informal education in the reading, writing and pronunciation of the Arabic language as part of their religious education.
</p><p>Other languages and dialects spoken include <a href="/wiki/Kedayan_Malay" class="mw-redirect" title="Kedayan Malay">Kedayan Malay</a> dialect, <a href="/wiki/Tutong_language" title="Tutong language">Tutong Malay dialect</a>, <a href="/wiki/Murutic_languages" title="Murutic languages">Murut</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Dusun_language" title="Dusun language">Dusun</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Martin_176-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Martin-176">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Brunei" title="Culture of Brunei">Culture of Brunei</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Royal_Regalie_(1).JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Royal_Regalie_%281%29.JPG/220px-Royal_Regalie_%281%29.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="168" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Royal_Regalie_%281%29.JPG/330px-Royal_Regalie_%281%29.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Royal_Regalie_%281%29.JPG/440px-Royal_Regalie_%281%29.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3216" data-file-height="2461" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Royal_Regalie_(1).JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Royal Regalia Museum</div></div></div>
<p>The culture of Brunei is predominantly <a href="/wiki/Malay_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Malay people">Malay</a> (reflecting its ethnicity), with heavy influences from <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a>, but is seen as much more conservative than <a href="/wiki/Indonesia" title="Indonesia">Indonesia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Malaysia" title="Malaysia">Malaysia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup> Influences to Bruneian culture come from the Malay cultures of the <a href="/wiki/Malay_Archipelago" title="Malay Archipelago">Malay Archipelago</a>. Four periods of cultural influence have occurred: animist, <a href="/wiki/Hindu" class="mw-redirect" title="Hindu">Hindu</a>, Islamic, and Western. Islam had a very strong influence, and was adopted as Brunei's ideology and philosophy.<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As a <a href="/wiki/Sharia" title="Sharia">Sharia</a> country, the sale and public consumption of alcohol is banned.<sup id="cite_ref-Brunei_Tourism_Website_184-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Brunei_Tourism_Website-184">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> Non-Muslims are allowed to bring in a limited amount of alcohol from their point of embarkation overseas for their own private consumption.<sup id="cite_ref-bruneipress.com.bn_111-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bruneipress.com.bn-111">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Media_of_Brunei" class="mw-redirect" title="Media of Brunei">Media of Brunei</a></div>
<p>Media in Brunei are said to be pro-government; press criticism of the government and monarchy is rare. The country ranks "Not Free" in media by <a href="/wiki/Freedom_House" title="Freedom House">Freedom House</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup> Nonetheless, the press is not overtly hostile toward alternative viewpoints and is not restricted to publishing only articles regarding the government. The government allowed a printing and publishing company, Brunei Press PLC, to form in 1953. The company continues to print the English daily <i><a href="/wiki/Borneo_Bulletin" title="Borneo Bulletin">Borneo Bulletin</a></i>. This paper began as a weekly <a href="/wiki/Community_paper" title="Community paper">community paper</a> and became a daily in 1990<sup id="cite_ref-bruneipress.com.bn_111-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bruneipress.com.bn-111">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> Apart from The <i>Borneo Bulletin</i>, there is also the <i><a href="/wiki/Media_Permata" title="Media Permata">Media Permata</a></i> and Pelita Brunei, the local Malay newspapers which are circulated daily. <i><a href="/wiki/The_Brunei_Times" title="The Brunei Times">The Brunei Times</a></i> is another English independent newspaper published in Brunei since 2006.<sup id="cite_ref-186" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-186">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Brunei government owns and operates three television channels with the introduction of digital TV using DVB-T (RTB Perdana, RTB Aneka and RTB Sukmaindera) and five radio stations (National FM, Pilihan FM, Nur Islam FM, Harmony FM and Pelangi FM). A private company has made cable television available (Astro-Kristal) as well as one private radio station, Kristal FM.<sup id="cite_ref-bruneipress.com.bn_111-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bruneipress.com.bn-111">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
It also has an online campus radio station, <i><a href="/wiki/UBD_FM" title="UBD FM">UBD FM</a></i>, that streams from its first university, <a href="/wiki/Universiti_Brunei_Darussalam" title="Universiti Brunei Darussalam">Universiti Brunei Darussalam</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sport">Sport</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sport_in_Brunei" title="Sport in Brunei">Sport in Brunei</a></div>
<p>The most popular sport in Brunei is <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">association football</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Brunei_national_football_team" title="Brunei national football team">Brunei national football team</a> joined <a href="/wiki/FIFA" title="FIFA">FIFA</a> in 1969, but has not had much success. The top two football leagues are the <a href="/wiki/Brunei_Super_League" title="Brunei Super League">Brunei Super League</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Brunei_Premier_League" title="Brunei Premier League">Brunei Premier League</a>.
</p><p>Brunei debuted <a href="/wiki/Brunei_at_the_Olympics" title="Brunei at the Olympics">at the Olympics</a> in 1996; it has competed at all subsequent <a href="/wiki/Summer_Olympics" class="mw-redirect" title="Summer Olympics">Summer Olympics</a> except 2008. The country has competed in badminton, shooting, swimming, and track-and-field, but has yet to win any medals. Brunei has had slightly more success <a href="/wiki/Brunei_at_the_Asian_Games" title="Brunei at the Asian Games">at the Asian Games</a>, winning four bronze medals. The first major international sporting event to be hosted in Brunei was the <a href="/wiki/1999_Southeast_Asian_Games" title="1999 Southeast Asian Games">1999 Southeast Asian Games</a>. According to the <a href="/wiki/All-time_Southeast_Asian_Games_medal_table" class="mw-redirect" title="All-time Southeast Asian Games medal table">all-time Southeast Asian Games medal table</a>, Bruneian athletes have won a total of 14 gold, 55 silver and 163 bronze medals at the games.
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-9"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-9">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">There is a <a href="/wiki/Legislative_Council_of_Brunei" title="Legislative Council of Brunei">Legislative Council</a>, which has no legislative power.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> As its role is only consultative it is not considered to be a legislature.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-16"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-16">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">In <a href="/wiki/Malay_language" title="Malay language">Malay</a>, the official name of Brunei is <i>Negara Brunei Darussalam</i>, literal meaning "Nation&#160;of Brunei, the&#160;Abode of Peace", but in <a href="/wiki/English_language" title="English language">English</a>, the official name of the country is always written as <i>Brunei Darussalam</i>, not written in the translated sense.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

Archived from <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.filipiniana.net/publication/letter-from-francisco-de-sande-to-felipe-ii/12791881675822">the original</a> on 27 March 2012<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">17 October</span> 2009</span>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.jtitle=filipiniana.net&amp;rft.atitle=Letter+from+Francisco+de+Sande+to+Felipe+II%2C+1578&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.filipiniana.net%2Fpublication%2Fletter-from-francisco-de-sande-to-felipe-ii%2F12791881675822&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ABrunei" class="Z3988"></span></span>

<li id="cite_note-56"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-56">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.gutenberg.org/files/16086/16086-h/16086-h.htm">Letter from Fajardo to Felipe III From Manila, August 15 1620. (From the Spanish Archives of the Indies)</a>("The infantry does not amount to two hundred men, in three companies. If these men were that number, and Spaniards, it would not be so bad; but, although I have not seen them, because they have not yet arrived here, I am told that they are, as at other times, for the most part boys, mestizos, and mulattoes, with some Indians (Native Americans). There is no little cause for regret in the great sums that reënforcements of such men waste for, and cost, your Majesty. I cannot see what betterment there will be until your Majesty shall provide it, since I do not think, that more can be done in Nueva Spaña, although the viceroy must be endeavoring to do so, as he is ordered.")</span>
</li>
